# BUILD_LOG.md — append-only

One entry per build, newest at the bottom. Claude Code appends an entry at the end of every build
(see .claude/skills/milestone-build/SKILL.md, Phase 6). Never rewrite past entries; add corrections
as new entries.

## Entry template

### B<x.y> — <title>
- **Status:** in progress | complete | blocked
- **Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
- **Summary:** two or three sentences.
- **Key files:** ...
- **Schema changes:** none | ...
- **Decisions:** none | D<n> ...
- **Deviations from spec:** none | ...
- **Owner actions requested:** ...
- **Owner confirmed:** yes/no (date)
- **Next:** B<next>

---

<!-- entries start below -->

### B0.1 — Repository scaffold
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-07
- **Summary:** Created the monorepo at `~/lp-platform` (git initialised, nothing committed) from the handoff zip. pnpm workspace with `apps/web` (Next.js 16.4 App Router, TS strict, Tailwind v4 via `@tailwindcss/postcss`, ESLint 9 flat config with import guard rails, Prettier, Vitest) and `services/worker` (Python 3.12, uv, FastAPI `GET /health`, ruff, strict mypy, pytest). `pnpm dev` runs both; typecheck, lint and test are clean.
- **Key files:** `package.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `.gitignore`, `apps/web/{package.json,next.config.ts,eslint.config.mjs,tsconfig.json,app/*}`, `services/worker/{pyproject.toml,app/main.py,tests/test_health.py}`, both `.env.example` files, CLAUDE.md §2 and §5.
- **Schema changes:** none.
- **Decisions:** D29 (repo root `~/lp-platform`), D30 (TypeScript 6.0.x), D31 (`LP_MOCK_LLM=1` dev default). CLAUDE.md §2 renumbered to match DECISIONS.md and Default decisions marked.
- **Deviations from spec:** Tailwind via `@tailwindcss/postcss` (as listed) rather than create-next-app 16.4's new `@tailwindcss/turbopack` default; Cache Components and Partial Prefetching left on (Next 16.4 defaults); `apps/web/AGENTS.md` kept (managed and regenerated by `next dev`); worker suite has one health test instead of being empty (pytest exits 5 on an empty suite); web test runs with `--passWithNoTests` until B0.3.
- **Incident:** during verification, port 3000 was held by another project's `next dev` (a `~/Folio*` site, PID 5129); the cleanup step killed it. No files touched; the owner needs to restart it. Verification was re-run with a cleanup that only stops processes under `~/lp-platform`.
- **Owner actions requested:** free disk space before B0.2; install Docker Desktop (Intel) before B0.2; restart the Folio dev server; reopen VS Code at `~/lp-platform`; optional first commit.
- **Fixes after the owner's second look (dark-mode screenshot):** the header was 52 px with a 14 px medium title and the panel 8 px inset; now 48 px, 13 px regular, 6 px, and the sidebar's top row lines up with the title. A brand mark's letter followed the theme instead of the brand colour (a cream brand showed a blank square in dark mode); it now uses fixed on-light/on-dark tokens. The Home cards' image areas went flat black in dark mode; they now keep a dark tint of their mark colour.
- **Owner request (third look):** the getting-started checklist moved from Home into the sidebar as a card above Settings (`components/app/sidebar-setup.tsx`: progress ring, title, "N/5 steps completed", steps as links; folds with a chevron, remembered in the browser; hidden once complete), like the reference and Notion's onboarding cards. `components/features/home/getting-started.tsx` removed.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B0.2 — Supabase, schema, auth

### B0.2 — Supabase, schema, auth
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-07
- **Summary:** Local Supabase stack (realtime, edge functions, analytics, image transforms and vector buckets off to save RAM) with the reviewed schema as migration `20261007132728_init.sql`, RLS, explicit table privileges and four storage buckets. Sign up, sign in, magic link and sign out work through server actions; signing up creates the user's org with them as owner; a session-refresh `proxy.ts` redirects by sign-in state; `/home` shows the user and their orgs.
- **Key files:** `supabase/{config.toml,migrations/20261007132728_init.sql,seed.sql,tests/database/tenant_isolation.test.sql}`, `apps/web/proxy.ts`, `apps/web/lib/{env.ts,supabase/*,auth/*,validators/auth.ts}`, `apps/web/components/features/auth/*`, `apps/web/app/{(auth),(app)/home,auth}/*`, `apps/web/types/database.ts` (generated).
- **Schema changes (from the draft):** explicit table grants (Supabase no longer grants read/write to API roles by default; anon gets nothing; TRUNCATE/TRIGGER/REFERENCES revoked); `orgs` updatable only on name/slug (D33); notifications read + mark-read only; `builds.template_version`; job dedupe index scoped per org; `allocate_build_key` not executable by users; all functions pin `search_path`; storage buckets and policies with a cast-safe `is_org_member_path()`.
- **Decisions:** D32 (publishable/secret keys), D33 (platform-controlled org settings), D34 (invites deferred to B1.6), D35 (server actions + zod until shadcn).
- **Deviations from spec:** `/` redirects to `/home` (placeholder page removed); magic link is for existing accounts only (new accounts sign up so their org gets a name); minimum password length 8 (config and form); `db:start` skips the CLI health gate on this machine.
- **Verification:** pgTAP 20/20; browser e2e 15/15 (redirects, sign-up, sign-out, wrong password, magic link via Mailpit, second user isolated, bad link); typecheck, lint, tests and `next build` clean.
- **Owner actions requested:** try sign-up in the browser; optional commit.
- **Fixes after the owner's second look (dark-mode screenshot):** the header was 52 px with a 14 px medium title and the panel 8 px inset; now 48 px, 13 px regular, 6 px, and the sidebar's top row lines up with the title. A brand mark's letter followed the theme instead of the brand colour (a cream brand showed a blank square in dark mode); it now uses fixed on-light/on-dark tokens. The Home cards' image areas went flat black in dark mode; they now keep a dark tint of their mark colour.
- **Owner request (third look):** the getting-started checklist moved from Home into the sidebar as a card above Settings (`components/app/sidebar-setup.tsx`: progress ring, title, "N/5 steps completed", steps as links; folds with a chevron, remembered in the browser; hidden once complete), like the reference and Notion's onboarding cards. `components/features/home/getting-started.tsx` removed.
- **Owner confirmed:** no

### B0.4a — Job system: worker API, queue, job loop, progress, notifications
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-08
- **Summary:** The worker now has settings from env, a Postgres pool, JWKS-verified auth with org membership checks, and `POST /jobs` (dedupe per org). A separate job loop claims jobs (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, 3 per worker, 2 per org, only kinds it knows), heartbeats every 10 s, requeues jobs whose heartbeat is 90 s old, retries crashes once, fails `UserFacingError`s straight away, pauses and hands back jobs on Ctrl+C, and writes progress events and a notification per finished job. `noop_demo` reports five steps and resumes after the last finished one. The web app has React Query, `/api/jobs` (start via the worker; read via Supabase RLS), `/api/notifications`, polling hooks, a jobs tray with live toasts, a real notifications bell, and a temporary demo-job card on Home. The shell's active-link reads moved into Suspense, so dynamic routes prerender a static shell (`instant = false` removed).
- **Key files:** `services/worker/{worker.py,app/{config,db,auth,log,notifications,main}.py,app/jobs/*}`, `services/worker/tests/{conftest,test_jobs,test_api}.py`; `apps/web/app/api/{jobs,notifications}/**`, `apps/web/lib/{api,jobs,queries}/*`, `apps/web/lib/validators/jobs.ts`, `apps/web/lib/auth/{session,api-session}.ts`, `apps/web/components/app/{job-tray,notifications-button,toaster,query-provider,with-pathname}.tsx`, `apps/web/components/features/home/demo-job-card.tsx`, root `package.json` (`dev:jobs`).
- **Schema changes:** none.
- **Decisions:** D42–D45 and O6 (approved with the plan), D46 (kind-filtered claims, graceful hand-back, resume).
- **Deviations from spec:** the per-org limit is soft (two workers claiming at the same instant can exceed it by one); signed-out `/api/*` calls get a 401 JSON instead of the sign-in redirect; the demo job's steps are 2 s each.
- **Verification:** worker ruff/mypy/pytest 28/28 (DB tests on local Supabase); web typecheck, lint, token check, vitest 52/52, `next build` clean; browser checks B0.4a 22/22 (live progress, tray, toasts, notifications, failure path, tenant isolation, graceful restart → resume), hard kill → requeued after 95 s → resumed on attempt 2; B0.3a shell 26/26 and B0.3b 46/46 after the shell change.
- **Owner actions requested:** try the demo job on Home; create the Anthropic key before B0.4b.
- **Fixes after the owner's second look (dark-mode screenshot):** the header was 52 px with a 14 px medium title and the panel 8 px inset; now 48 px, 13 px regular, 6 px, and the sidebar's top row lines up with the title. A brand mark's letter followed the theme instead of the brand colour (a cream brand showed a blank square in dark mode); it now uses fixed on-light/on-dark tokens. The Home cards' image areas went flat black in dark mode; they now keep a dark tint of their mark colour.
- **Owner request (third look):** the getting-started checklist moved from Home into the sidebar as a card above Settings (`components/app/sidebar-setup.tsx`: progress ring, title, "N/5 steps completed", steps as links; folds with a chevron, remembered in the browser; hidden once complete), like the reference and Notion's onboarding cards. `components/features/home/getting-started.tsx` removed.

### B0.4b — Providers, cost table, ledger
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-08
- **Summary:** One provider module per vendor, all recording ledger rows inside a cost context set by the job runner: Anthropic (official SDK 1.x, model by role from settings, mock by default with $0 rows, SDK retries, structured outputs and effort via `output_config`, server-side `fallbacks: "default"` for Sonnet 5.5 and other supporting models with each attempt priced at its own model's rates, refusals recorded then raised), fal (mock "MOCK IMAGE" JPEGs by default; real client wired, not exercised; locked/unfunded errors set a 10-minute org lock and later calls fail fast), Supabase Storage over REST with content-hash asset dedupe, one pricing table, and the provider lock helpers. The demo job now makes a mock AI call and a mock image saved to storage, and the Home card shows its 2 ledger rows at $0.00 and the image. One live Claude Haiku 5.5 call (owner's go) returned "LP Platform key check OK": 25 in / 11 out tokens, $0.000008, recorded in the owner's ledger.
- **Key files:** `services/worker/app/providers/{anthropic,fal,storage,cost,ledger,locks,smoke}.py`, `app/config.py`, `app/jobs/{runner.py,kinds/noop_demo.py}`, `tests/test_providers.py`; `supabase/migrations/20261008123003_widen_cost_precision.sql`; `apps/web/components/features/home/demo-job-card.tsx`, `apps/web/lib/{validators/jobs,jobs/queries}.ts`.
- **Schema changes:** `ledger_entries.cost_usd` and `builds.cost_usd` widened from `numeric(10,4)` to `numeric(12,6)` (found by the live check: the $0.000008 call was stored as $0).
- **Decisions:** D47 (6-decimal costs; provider calls require a cost context; UI/demo never call paid providers).
- **Deviations from spec:** no extra retry wrapper around the SDK (its 2 retries with backoff cover 429/5xx/connection errors; the copy pass adds its own per-chunk attempts in B0.6); fal request arguments to be checked against fal's current schema at B1.3; pgTAP test 19 now counts only its own test orgs (it assumed an empty database).
- **Verification:** worker ruff/mypy/pytest 46/46; web typecheck, lint, token check, vitest 52/52, `next build`; pgTAP 20/20; browser check 24/24 (incl. demo ledger line and stored image); live key check passed. Not run: `supabase db reset` (would wipe the owner's local account); the migration applied cleanly with `supabase migration up --local`.
- **Owner actions requested:** none required; optional review of the Home demo card.
- **Fixes after the owner's second look (dark-mode screenshot):** the header was 52 px with a 14 px medium title and the panel 8 px inset; now 48 px, 13 px regular, 6 px, and the sidebar's top row lines up with the title. A brand mark's letter followed the theme instead of the brand colour (a cream brand showed a blank square in dark mode); it now uses fixed on-light/on-dark tokens. The Home cards' image areas went flat black in dark mode; they now keep a dark tint of their mark colour.
- **Owner request (third look):** the getting-started checklist moved from Home into the sidebar as a card above Settings (`components/app/sidebar-setup.tsx`: progress ring, title, "N/5 steps completed", steps as links; folds with a chevron, remembered in the browser; hidden once complete), like the reference and Notion's onboarding cards. `components/features/home/getting-started.tsx` removed.

### B0.5a — Brands and "Fetch from URL"
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-08
- **Summary:** Brands list, New brand (paste a URL → live fetch steps → review the draft with an AffectUI colour recommendation → save; or fill in by hand), and the brand page with Overview, Products (B0.5b), Pages and Brand DNA tabs (every field editable). The worker's `brand_autofetch` job loads the page (plain GET, then Playwright), reads it (name, tagline, logo, favicon, theme colour, language, email, buttons, text), reads the rendered page (computed colours and fonts), picks the palette (leaving out greys and third-party widget colours) and writes the profile with one AI call (mock by default). Saving queues `brand_assets`, which downloads the logo and favicon into storage. An SSRF guard covers every fetch. The demo job and brand fetch show their steps with the progress tracker (spinner and shimmer for the running step, strike-through when done, a cross and the reason when failed).
- **Tested on the owner's sites (mock AI):** Allbirds (name, ALL, cream #ece9e2 with navy #090c43 as alternative, Geograph, logo), Graza (GRAZA, GRA, lime #d1e030, ITC Garamond Condensed / GT Alpina Typewriter, logo, email), Topicals (TOP, red #f61f1f, Chalet, inline-SVG logo).

### Motion pass — emil-design-eng skill
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-09
- **Summary:** Applied Emil Kowalski's design-engineering skill to all UI motion: strong custom curves as tokens (`--ease-out`, `--ease-in-out`, `--ease-drawer`); buttons scale to 0.97 when pressed with named transition properties; menus and popovers 150 ms in, 100 ms out, growing from their trigger; tooltips wait 400 ms, then neighbours open instantly; sheets use the drawer curve; list groups open without a height animation; spinners turn faster (0.7 s); AffectUI components use our curve with timings cut to 300 ms or less; reduced motion is gentler, not removed.
- **Key files:** `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `components/ui/{button,badge,dropdown-menu,popover,tooltip,sheet,progress,progress-tracker,profile-menu,filter-menu,smart-recommendation}.tsx`, `components/app/{grouped-list,sidebar}.tsx`, spinners in `job-tray`, `form-status`, `brand-form`; `.claude/skills/emil-design-eng/` and `skills-lock.json`.
- **Decisions:** D51 (motion standard; amends D48). DESIGN.md §6 motion rules rewritten. Skill licence (MIT) in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.
- **Verification:** computed styles checked in the running app (button curve and 0.97 press, popover 150 ms with the custom curve, tracker 240 ms, spinner token); vitest, lint, token check; browser suites B0.4c, B0.4a, B0.3b pass.

### B0.5b — Products
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-09
- **Summary:** The brand's Products tab: paste a product page and a `product_pull` job reads the store's product data (Shopify JSON, catalogue, selling plans; no Accept-Language) or, failing that, the page's JSON-LD, share tags or heading; stores up to 8 images; writes the description, selling points, specs and selling context with one AI call; and saves the product (adding it again updates it). The product page shows the price, images, options, subscriptions and an editable form, with Pull again and Remove. A `product_briefing` job writes the wizard briefing (four audiences, benefits, features, objections), cached and refreshable. Real AI is on (mock is still forced in tests).
- **Tested on the owner's products (real AI):** Allbirds Wool Runner ($110, 7 sizes, 5 images, store data), Graza Drizzle ($21, 4 bottle options with bundle prices, 3 subscription prices at $19.95, store data), Topicals Faded serum ($38, 3 sizes from the page's product data, 2 images). Cost of the run: $0.089 (3 brand profiles $0.023, 4 product write-ups $0.038, 3 briefings $0.027).
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/product_extract.py`, `app/jobs/kinds/{product_pull,product_briefing}.py`, `app/models/product.py`, `app/providers/anthropic.py` (`strict_schema`), `app/jobs/registry.py` + `app/main.py` (dedupe for paid jobs), tests `test_product_engines.py`, `test_product_jobs.py` and fixtures; web `app/(app)/brands/[brandId]/products/**`, `components/features/products/*`, `lib/products/{queries,actions,form}.ts`, `lib/validators/product.ts`.
- **Schema changes:** none.
- **Decisions:** D52 (product pull and briefing rules).
- **Fixes found while testing:** forms no longer show blank fields until the page's JavaScript loads (values are in the server HTML; Brand DNA too), and saving no longer resets what you type straight afterwards; brand list product counts skip removed products.
- **Deviations from spec:** pulled products save directly (approved plan); Remove (soft delete) and Pull again added so a wrong pull can be fixed; no image upload or reorder yet; connected-store (Admin API) pulls wait for the Shopify connection (B1.5).
- **Verification:** worker ruff, mypy, pytest 117/117; web typecheck, lint, token check, prettier, vitest 96/96; browser check B0.5b 40/42 on real sites with real AI (the 2 misses were the test typing before the form loaded, which led to the form fix; the edit flow then passed 12/12 at full speed); B0.4c, B0.4a, B0.3b pass.

### B0.6a — Pipeline and page assembly (no AI)
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-09
- **Summary:** The build pipeline runs end to end as a LangGraph graph (owned path; clone-only nodes are stubs never reached by owned builds). From the test template it produces a page with the brand's colours and fonts, logo, product options, prices, selling points, product photos and free placeholder images, removes forbidden terms, finishes meta/share tags/favicon/emails/CTAs, and runs the claims check. Each build is checkpointed after every node and resumes after a worker restart without redoing work. Builds start from a temporary "Build a test page" button on the product page (or `pnpm smoke:build`); the build page shows live steps, a sandboxed preview (desktop / 390 px mobile) and the report. The Pages list now shows real builds; the demo-job card is gone. Content pages are centred (owner request, logged separately).
- **Key files:** worker `app/pipeline/{graph,state,runtime,checkpoint}.py`, `app/pipeline/nodes/*` (one module per node), `app/engines/{html,sections,recolor,brand_parts,purge,images,finalize,compliance,compliance_rules,money}.py`, `app/jobs/kinds/build.py`, `app/builds.py`, `app/main.py` (POST /builds), `app/cli/{seed_library,smoke_build}.py`, `app/models/{template,build}.py`; `templates/library/advertorial-fixture/`; web `app/(app)/pages/[buildId]/page.tsx`, `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts`, `components/features/builds/*`, `components/features/products/test-build-button.tsx`, `lib/builds/*`, `lib/validators/build.ts`, `lib/hooks/use-page-refresh.ts`.

- **New dependencies:** `langgraph` 1.2.14, `langgraph-checkpoint-postgres` 3.1.2 (already listed in DEPENDENCIES.md).
- **Decisions:** D56 (checkpoints and page revisions), D57 (builds created by the worker; test builds use the library template directly), D58 (markup contract additions).
- **Fixes found while testing:** sections reorder within their container (the test template wraps its middle sections in `<main>`); the hue sweep no longer tints slate text colours; a finished node is recorded even while the worker is stopping, so resume never redoes it; a discount promo (and its empty bar) goes for brands without a sale; the test template uses `--lp-on-*` tokens for text on brand colours (Allbirds' cream primary made white text unreadable).

### B0.6b — Copy rewrite and export
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-09
- **Summary:** The copy pass rewrites every rewritable text fragment of the page with Claude in the brand's voice (40 per call, up to 4 calls at once, 3 attempts each, up to two shortening passes for lines over their length budget, strict re-prompts for clones, drift-checked write-back). Locked slots, bylines and labels never change; a failed chunk keeps the template's text and sets a copy error that will block publish, without failing the build. The build page shows how many lines were rewritten, a "Copy changes" list, and mock/error banners. Export HTML produces one self-contained file (images, icons and CSS inlined, pipeline markers stripped) with a Download button; other orgs get 404.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/{fragments,copy_rules,export}.py`, `app/pipeline/nodes/copy_rewrite.py`, `app/jobs/kinds/export.py`, `app/engines/finalize.py` (`fill_names`), `app/engines/html.py` (`style_text`); web `components/features/builds/{export-button,build-report}.tsx`, `app/api/builds/[buildId]/export/route.ts`, `lib/validators/build.ts`.

- **Decisions:** D59 (copy pass and export details).
- **Fixes found while testing:** BeautifulSoup's `get_text()` returns nothing for a `<style>` whose CSS was replaced in the same pass, which emptied inlined stylesheets; styles are now read directly (`style_text`), in export and recolour. Promo lines that finalize replaces or removes are no longer rewritten.

- **Verification:** worker ruff, mypy, pytest 181/181 (15 new: fragments, budgets, protection, reply salvage, prompt order, mock replies, export inlining; pipeline copy pass, failed chunk, shortening pass, export job); web typecheck, lint, token check, vitest 99/99; browser check B0.6b 19/19 in mock mode (dev servers run with LP_MOCK_LLM=1 so tests cost nothing). Cost: $0.

Testimonials are rewritten like the rest of the copy; the owner decided to keep it that way, without labels, and to pull real reviews later (D60).

### B1.2 — Create wizard
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-09
- **Summary:** `/pages/new` is the create wizard, laid out like the previous tool's "Create a funnel": steps on the left, the step in the middle (Back, progress dots, Continue), and the brand dossier on the right (product photo, price, template, briefing status and ticked facts). The six steps are Brand & product, Template, Imagery, Audience, Emphasis, and Review & build. Every choice is kept in the URL: a refresh or a shared link keeps it, the browser's Back goes back a step, and steps can't be skipped. If the product has no briefing yet, it starts writing in the background as soon as the product is chosen. Build page creates the build with the full payload and opens the build page with its live steps. The worker stores a targeting record on the build. The author voice goes into the copy instructions, and an expert author never claims credentials; the claims check flags any it finds. The sidebar's New page, "New page for this product" on the product page and "Create a page" in the template editor all open the wizard with their choice made. The temporary test-build buttons are gone.

### T2 — Code renamed to the Figma token names (D68)
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-10
- **Summary:** The web app now uses the LP Figma file's token names and values. `apps/web/app/globals.css` is generated from the Figma export (121 primitives as plain variables, 92 colour tokens with 50 dark overrides, type, radius, sizes, shadows); Tailwind's default type and radius scales are off. A script renamed 508 colour, 33 status-text, 242 type and 23 radius classes across 82 files (`bg-surface` → `bg-surface-primary`, `text-fg-muted` → `text-sub-text`, `text-sm` → `text-type-caption-1`, `rounded-xs` → `rounded-xsm` …); accent uses were assigned by role (fills, borders and focus → Spot-500-Indigo; text on the indigo tint → Text-Link; numbers on solid fills → On-Dark; primary hover → Button-Primary-Active; destructive button → Button-Danger tokens); status icons on plain surfaces use Spot colours, status text and icons on tints use Text-*; inputs focus with Stroke-Input-Active; alerts and field errors use Text-Danger. shadcn and AffectUI mappings repointed (menus and popovers on Surface-Modal). `cn()` knows the new type and radius names. `pnpm lint:tokens` now also fails on retired names (`scripts/check-token-names.mjs`), including in `components/ui`. DESIGN.md §4 rewritten with a before/after name table.
- **Figma changes in this build:** Text-Danger dark → Red 50 (`#f9dddd`); Radius-L code syntax `var(--radius-lg)`; Base primitives' code syntax `--base-white`/`--base-black`; H4 text styles 30 px Crimson Pro; text style descriptions name their code class.
- **Key files:** `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `apps/web/lib/utils.ts` (+ test), 80 component and route files, `scripts/check-token-names.mjs`, `scripts/check-tokens.sh`, `docs/DESIGN.md` §4, `docs/DECISIONS.md` (D68), `CLAUDE.md` §5.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D68 (T2 note).
- **Deviations from plan:** type classes are `text-type-*` (not `text-caption-1`) and Radius-L is `--radius-lg` in code, both to avoid Tailwind name clashes; Text-Danger dark differs from Quality for readability; H4 became the 30 px serif title style (the sign-in title used Tailwind's `text-3xl`, which isn't in the Figma scale).
- **Verification:** web typecheck, ESLint, token check and token-name check (and a probe file proving the name check fails on old names), Prettier, vitest 112/112 (one run timed out under load; the file and the full suite then passed), `next build`. Figma ↔ CSS parity script: 121 primitives and 92 colour tokens identical in light and dark. Screenshots of 24 screens in light and dark before and after, pixel-compared against the production build: light mode identical on 17 screens, under 0.2% on 7 (all status-text colour changes; Pages and sidebar differences were row times and a notification count loading); dark mode changed as planned (menus, dividers, chips, selected tints, alerts). The shared dev server kept serving stale code after a stash/restore, so the final screenshots came from a production build on :3005 (stopped afterwards). Cost: $0.

- **Summary:** `components/app/header-popover.tsx` (new) holds the shared frame, title, footer, footer action and `PopoverScroll` (the list, up to 384 px, with the fade shown only while more sits below). Notifications and Jobs both use it. Fade: `popover-fade` (gradient into `--color-popover-fade` plus a 7.5 px backdrop blur masked to grow downwards); light grey-10, dark a 60/40 mix of grey-900 and grey-950 (#1c1c1c-ish), picked from #121212, #181818, #1c1c1c and #212121 side by side. Footer: `divider-t-half`, `hover:shadow-lift`. Jobs read state: `POST /api/jobs/seen` (auth `user_metadata.jobs_seen_at`), `seenAt` on `GET /api/jobs`, `useMarkJobsSeen` (optimistic); the Jobs dot shows while jobs run or any finished since. Header dots and counts render after hydration (`lib/use-hydrated.ts`): a hydration mismatch on the dot was seen once during testing. Filter and Display: no fill until hover.

- **Deviations from the design:** the fade is stronger than Figma's (Figma's mask is mostly hidden under the opaque footer; here the 32 px above the footer fade and blur so scrolling content visibly dissolves), and it only shows while the list can scroll further; dark row hover uses Foreground-02; jobs rows have no per-item unread dot (the frame shows status icons there), so "unread" drives the header dot and the footer action.

Cost: $0.

(2) Notifications/Jobs blur rebuilt after researching how progressive blur is done on the web (no native gradient blur; the standard is stacked backdrop-filter layers with doubling radii in overlapping masks plus a tint, as in Magic UI's ProgressiveBlur and Cult UI's EdgeBlur): `progressive-blur` utility with five layers (0.5/1/2/4/8 px) over the list's last 40 px, `-webkit-` backdrop and mask prefixes, an eased tint, hidden with `visibility` (Safari drops backdrop blur under a parent opacity). Checked at 2x in light and dark on both popovers. (3) Floating frames lighter: `edge-hairline` + `shadow-float` on the workspace, account, row and Display menus, header popovers and toasts; workspace menu without the inner edge shadow. Verification: typecheck, ESLint, token checks, Prettier, vitest 124/124. Cost: $0.
- **Follow-up 3 (owner, same day):** stroke switched from Nucleo to Quality. The page needs the owner's login, but the stylesheet is public: the file in the owner's DevTools (`index-BGHD8ka6.css`) is gone after a deploy, so values came from the current one (`index-kXlYR7RX.css`): `--Border-on-bg` = Grey 50 #e6e6e6 (light) / Grey 400 #2a2a2a (dark), used as `border-[0.5px] border-border-on-bg` on the content panel and the sticky header. `--color-edge` → Stroke-Strong, highlight removed, `edge` is now a plain 0.5 px border. (3) Notification/Jobs blur replaced by a fade after research (shadcn scroll-fade, tw-fade, ngrok Mantle, pqina): `scroll-fade-b` mask driven by `animation-timeline: scroll(self block)`; measured in Chromium: 40 px at the top, 20 px with 20 px left, 0 px at the end; `data-more` fallback for Firefox. ### Security update — Next.js and dependency vulnerabilities (owner request)
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Owner:** fix all the Next.js issues, upgrade and fix all.
- **Found:** `pnpm audit`: 17 findings (2 critical, 6 high): Next.js 16.4.0 had two unauthenticated remote code execution flaws (Image Optimizer, `use cache`) and a Server Actions denial of service, fixed in 16.4.1; Zod 4.6.5 and shadcn's Zod 3 (memory exhaustion); and through the shadcn CLI, jsdom and the form resolver: undici, body-parser, ip-address, fast-uri. `pip-audit` on the worker: Pillow 12.3.0 (37), langgraph-checkpoint-postgres 3.1.2, certifi.
- **Summary:** web: `next` and `eslint-config-next` 16.4.3, `zod` ^4.6.7, `shadcn` ^4.22.1 (its Zod 3 is gone), `jsdom` ^30.1.3; undici, body-parser, ip-address and fast-uri refreshed within their parents' ranges (no overrides). Worker: `pillow>=13.0.0`, `langgraph-checkpoint-postgres>=3.1.3` (held at the 3.1 patch, which brings `langgraph-checkpoint` 4.3.0; it adds no checkpoint migrations: 10 in the package, the database has 0–9), certifi 2026.10.23. Both audits now report no known vulnerabilities.
- **Verification:** web typecheck, ESLint, token checks, Prettier, vitest 132/132, `next build`; worker ruff, ruff format, mypy, pytest 214 passed. In the browser on the upgraded dev server with a throwaway account and seeded builds (no pipeline run): Home, Pages, Templates, Brands, a brand and its products, the wizard, a finished and a failed build page, /dev/build-states and Settings all render with no page errors. ### B1.4a — Review desk: edit, undo, approve
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation)
- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Owner:** approved the B1.4 plan with every recommendation (split into B1.4a and B1.4b; build in the Build page's style; Needs review is the draft and edits send an approved page back; edit in the panel; approving with open claims allowed; real redrafts only on the owner's go). D83, D84.
- **Summary:** a finished build's page is now the review desk. The header shows the status and Approve / Back to review (with a warning when claims are marked Fix). The right panel has Overview (today's report plus Your choices), Copy (every line by section, searchable; a click here or on the line in the preview opens it; Save snapshots the page, re-checks the claims, marks the export out of date and reloads the preview) and History (Undo, and Restore for any of the last 12 changes). Claims carry the line they sit in: Edit line opens it, the section button outlines it. Any change to an approved page sends it back to Needs review; a change made on an outdated page (another tab) is refused with Reload.
- **Key files:** worker `app/desk.py` (snapshots, edits, status, undo, restore), `app/models/desk.py`, `app/engines/proof.py` (marks each line for the preview), `app/engines/compliance.py` (findings carry their fragment), `app/main.py` (GET /builds/{id}/desk and /proof; POST /builds/{id}/copy, /status, /undo, /restore), `tests/test_desk.py`; web `components/features/builds/{desk-context,desk-panel,status-actions}.tsx`, `build-preview.tsx` (edit mode and the bridge messages), `build-report.tsx` (Edit line, line-level locate), `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts` (`?mode=edit` with the desk's bridge script), `app/api/builds/[buildId]/desk/[action]/route.ts`, `lib/builds/desk.ts`, `lib/validators/desk.ts`, `lib/api/{worker,client}.ts`, `components/ui/tabs.tsx` (segmented variant), `app/(app)/dev/build-states` (states 8–11), tests `lib/builds/desk.test.ts`, `components/features/builds/desk-panel.test.tsx`; `supabase/tests/database/tenant_isolation.test.sql`; the figma-screen-build skill's cleanup also removes desk files.

### Fix — Job queues (tests vs the live worker)
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation; not committed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Owner:** go to fix the clash found while pushing B1.4a (two pipeline tests failed because Railway's job loop claimed their jobs).
- **Summary:** jobs carry a queue (`jobs.queue`, default `main`); a worker queues, claims and reaps only its own queue (`LP_JOB_QUEUE`, default `main`). The worker tests set a fresh `test-…` queue per run, so the live worker never reaps or runs their jobs (and so never makes real Claude calls on test data), and a test never claims a real job. D85. *Correction (same day, during B1.4b):* the two failures were the live job loop's reaper requeuing long test jobs (a test runs its job without the loop's heartbeat), not it claiming them: build and export tests use test-only kinds it can't run.
- **Key files:** `supabase/migrations/20261025045925_job_queues.sql`, `services/worker/app/config.py`, `app/jobs/queue.py`, `app/jobs/models.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_jobs.py` (new queue test); `.env.example`, `docs/DEPENDENCIES.md`, `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Schema changes:** `jobs.queue text not null default 'main'`; the claim index now leads with the queue. Applied to the cloud dev project; types regenerated.
- **Verification:** ruff, ruff format, mypy; job tests 18/18; every other worker test except the four files that queue real job kinds (pipeline, API, brand and product jobs) 174/174; web typecheck. Those four wait until Railway runs this code: the worker there now still claims every queue.
- **Owner confirmed:** no

### B1.5a — Publish gate and Vercel
- **Status:** complete (awaiting owner confirmation; not committed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Owner:** approved the plan (testing switch; one project per brand; pages public; publish from Approved, edits send a published page back to review; no Hobby-plan note; their own Vercel account) and asked for a central modal when the deployment is ready: the page on the left, basic details on the right, the publish status below beside a Shopify button, after researching how this works. D87.
- **Research:** a Vercel deployment is live only when `readyState` is READY *and* `aliasAssigned` is true (an `aliasError` means it failed); the job also opens the page's address before calling it done. Vercel Authentication is switched off per project with `PATCH /v9/projects/{id} {"ssoProtection": null}`. Files go to `/v2/files` by SHA-1, then `/v13/deployments` with `projectSettings.framework: null` and `target: production`. Vercel adds no X-Frame-Options to static pages, so the modal frames the live address itself. Success modals carry one primary action and the link to share (Open page, Copy).
- **Summary:** Settings → Integrations connects Vercel (token checked with Vercel, kept in Vault, last four shown; Replace, Disconnect) and holds the owner's testing switch. On an approved page, Publish asks first (Vercel, then what blocks it, Publish anyway); the job exports the page, finds or creates the brand's project (public), deploys every live page of the brand with this one, waits for READY with the address, checks it opens, records the publication and marks the page Published. A modal then opens with the live page, its details, Live on Vercel and Publish to Shopify (B1.5b); Live in the header reopens it; the Overview lists every publish. Edits send a published page back to Needs review.
- **Key files:** worker `app/vault.py`, `app/providers/vercel.py`, `app/publish/{gate,site,assets}.py`, `app/publishing.py`, `app/jobs/kinds/publish_vercel.py`, `app/models/publishing.py`, `app/main.py` (GET/POST/DELETE /integrations…, POST /orgs/{id}/testing, GET /builds/{id}/publishing, POST /builds/{id}/publish), `app/desk.py` (Published is editable; changes withdraw approval), `app/jobs/kinds/export.py` (resolver moved), tests `tests/test_publish.py`; web `components/features/builds/{publish-context,publish-controls}.tsx`, `components/features/settings/integrations.tsx`, `app/(app)/settings/page.tsx`, the build page, `desk-context.tsx` (`reload`), API routes (`/api/integrations`, `/api/integrations/vercel`, `/api/orgs/testing`, `/api/builds/[id]/publishing`, `/api/builds/[id]/publish`), `lib/validators/publishing.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `/dev/build-states` (states 16–19).
- **Schema changes:** `20261025134358_publishing.sql`: members can't write `publications`; `orgs.allow_placeholder_publish` (worker-written). Applied; types regenerated; pgTAP 25 checks (two new).
- **Decisions:** D87.
- **Deviations from spec:** placeholder images can be published while the owner's testing switch is on (the spec says never; the owner chose this until real images); every deployment carries all of the brand's live pages at `/<key>` (the spec says one deployment per publish, which would replace the brand's other pages).
- **Verification:** worker ruff, mypy, publish tests 10/10 (gate, site, Vault round trip, the Vercel client and the whole publish against a fake Vercel: login wall off, files, READY with alias, Published, a second page keeping the first, owners-only settings, edits back to review); web typecheck, ESLint, tokens, Prettier, vitest (4 new). In the browser (local web and worker; throwaway account): Settings shows Connect Vercel and the testing switch flips; on an approved page Publish asks for Vercel and lists the claim with Publish anyway; the published modal in light and dark on the states page. Not yet done: a real publish to Vercel (needs the owner's token).
- **Owner confirmed:** yes (2026-10-25: connected their Vercel account, asked to push)
- **Next:** B1.5b — Shopify: connect a store and publish a draft page
- **Follow-up (owner, same day): Settings like Quality's.** Settings now has its own sidebar in place of the app's (Back to app; Personal: Account; Workspace: General, Members, Usage, Billing, Trash; Tools: Connections; unbuilt ones show Soon), read from Quality's current app bundle (D88). `/settings` opens General (the workspace name; Testing with the placeholder switch). Connections shows Vercel and Shopify (Soon) as Quality-style cards; Connect opens a modal (the Vercel mark, Connect Vercel, the access token with a link to where tokens are made, an optional team id; the error in the footer if Vercel refuses it); a connected Vercel's View connection opens its page (connected on, account, connected by, Replace token, Disconnect, the brand projects with Open). The worker's integrations answer adds who connected Vercel and the brand projects. Checked in the browser in light and dark, including a wrong token's error from Vercel.

- **Fix (owner's first real connect, same day):** Vercel answered "User not found" to the token check. Newer Vercel accounts keep projects in a team ("name's projects"), and a token made for that team can't read the user, so the check now asks for the token's teams: the team typed in (its id or slug), else the token's only team, which publishing then uses (`?teamId=`); a personal token without a team still publishes to the user's account. Test added (11/11).

### Fix — The window scrolled away on long pages (2026-10-26)
- **Status:** complete (owner's report on a product page; not committed)

### Follow-up — Stacked modals as quick as Emil's (2026-10-28)
- **Status:** complete (owner: "these don’t feel as snappy as the video"; pushed)
- **Summary:** Emil's clip measured at its full 60 fps: a step settles in about 220 ms and starts at full speed (47 % of the way in 33 ms, 80 % in 85 ms); the new card is opaque in about 100 ms; a leaving card is gone in about 100 ms. Ours sprang for 320 ms (about 400 ms to settle), and a spring starts from rest, so it was only 12 % of the way at 30 ms and 57 % at 85 ms; the fade took 200 ms and the dimming 240 ms. Now the step moves on Emil's strong ease-out over 250 ms (`MOVE` in `modal-stack.tsx`), the new card fades in over 120 ms, a leaving card fades out over 100 ms, the blur clears in 150 ms and the card behind dims in 150 ms.
- **Verification:** sampled frame by frame in Chromium with GPU drawing (60 fps): push 38 % at 30 ms, 84 % at 81 ms, settled at about 200 ms; back: the front card gone in about 60 ms, the card behind settled in about 180 ms. The blur doesn't cost frames on the GPU. On the dev server the first push of a session spends about 400 ms rendering the connect form before anything moves (dev React; once per session); later pushes start within a frame. ### Follow-up — Speed pass (2026-10-28)
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go ahead"; pushed)
- **Summary:** three changes after the worker moved beside the database (D91). (1) The worker's pool keeps two connections open and skips its before-use test for a connection used within the last 30 s (`app/db.py`: `check_connection`, `note_returned`; idle ones are still tested, so a dropped connection is still replaced): one round trip fewer on almost every database use. (2) The build page asks the worker for the desk and for publishing together (`loadDeskAndPublishing`). (3) The desk preview's address carries the page's version and the build's last update (`?mode=edit&v=…&u=…`, which change with every edit and every rebuild; the pipeline's final write doesn't bump `page_version`, hence the second key), and the preview route lets the browser keep a versioned response for an hour (`private, max-age=3600`, `Vary: Cookie`); the plain preview (`?v=<updated_at>`) too.
- **Verification:** a worker read on the local worker (far from the database, so each round trip shows): 1,378 → 1,199 ms. On the live Railway worker, with a seeded throwaway build: the desk and publishing calls take 1,688 ms one after the other and 879 ms together, measured from here (from Vercel, beside the worker, the saving is about one call: ~0.4 s per opening of a built page). On localhost with the seeded build: the desk loads; leaving for Pages and coming back sent no new preview request (served from the browser's cache). Worker: pytest 273/273 (new `tests/test_db.py`), ruff, mypy; web: typecheck, lint, tokens, prettier, vitest 158/158. The throwaway account, build and file were deleted.

### B1.6a — Removing a Shopify draft, and Trash
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go ahead"; pushed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Summary:** the published modal's Shopify row gets Remove draft (asks first in the row; a job deletes the draft page from the store and records it as an unpublish; the next publish makes a new page). Pages, brands, products and templates move to Trash from their menus (asks first; Undo in the toast) and wait 7 days in Settings → Trash, drawn from Quality's Trash, where anyone restores and owners and admins delete for good; the job loop empties what's over 7 days hourly, with the stored files. Pages building or published can't be moved (unpublish first); a brand takes its pages and products with it.

- **Deviations from spec:** Trash keeps 7 days (BUILD_SPEC §8.6) and now covers pages, brands and products too (B1.6's "soft delete/restore"); Quality's per-item read-only page isn't built (Restore is the action). Products and templates deleted for good while pages use them are hidden, their rows kept (pages hold them with `on delete restrict`). Items deleted before today (old soft deletes) are swept like the rest once they're over 7 days.

### Fix — Publishing timed out saving the page (2026-10-28)
- **Status:** complete (owner: "i get a failed to publish to vercel message when i try to publish ALL-1"; pushed)
- **Summary:** three local publishes of ALL-1 failed with "Something went wrong": saving the exported page (every image inlined, megabytes) to storage hit httpx's default 5 s limit on a slow link from the local job loop. Storage calls now allow 15 s to connect, 2 min to read and 5 min to write (`storage.TIMEOUT`), network failures become `StorageError`, and the publish jobs (Vercel, its unpublish, Shopify) report them as "Couldn't save the page in storage (the connection was too slow or dropped). Try again." The live worker sits beside storage (D91), so it's mostly a local problem.
- **Verification:** the job loop's traceback (`httpx.WriteTimeout` in `storage.upload`, all three); a 6 MB upload from this machine (3.7 s, removed after); worker publish, Shopify and Trash tests 29/29, ruff, mypy.

### B2.1 — Fetch and clone
- **Status:** complete (owner: "for the test URLS: … and your recommendation"; not committed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-30
- **Summary:** the clone path's front half. Templates has **Clone a page**: a page's address or its pasted HTML, a name and a type. The first clone in a workspace asks an owner or admin to accept the clone terms (placeholder wording until legal review, O2); members can read them. A free `template_clone` job loads the page (the fetch ladder; the proxy step exists but is off), rejects bot checks and fragments, inlines the stylesheets, removes known trackers, fixes lazy and broken image addresses, makes every URL absolute, sets videos and Lottie aside as media slots, notes the platform, and flags store product pages. It saves the raw and cloned pages and a picture for the card, and the clone appears in Yours as "Cloned from <site>" with Preview. Building from a clone waits for its analysis (B2.2): the wizard leaves it out and the worker refuses.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/clone.py` (the pass), `app/jobs/kinds/template_clone.py` (the job), `app/clones.py` + `app/models/clone.py` (terms, starting a clone), `app/main.py` (`GET/POST /clone-terms`, `POST /templates/clone`, a 422 for builds from unanalysed clones), `app/builds.py`, `app/fetch/ladder.py` + `browser.py` (proxy step, guarded screenshot), `app/config.py` (`LP_FETCH_PROXY`, `LP_LIMIT_CLONES_PER_HOUR`), `app/jobs/queue.py` ("page clones" cap), `tests/test_clone.py`, `tests/test_clone_job.py`, `tests/fixtures/clone/*`; web `app/(app)/templates/clone/page.tsx`, `components/features/templates/clone-flow.tsx` (+ test), `clone-terms-dialog.tsx`, `template-card.tsx`, `app/(app)/templates/page.tsx` (Clone a page), `app/api/clone-terms/route.ts`, `app/api/templates/clone/route.ts`, `lib/validators/clone.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/templates/queries.ts`, `lib/wizard/queries.ts`, `app/preview/template/[templateId]/route.ts` (`<base href>` for clones), `components/app/select-field.tsx` (`labelClassName`), `lib/jobs/progress.ts` ("Page clone").

### B2.2 — Analysis
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go ahead"; not committed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-30
- **Summary:** every clone is analysed as soon as it's saved: one Claude call reads a digest of the page and names the source's colours, brand words, logos, buy links and variant pickers, picks its sections with a role and name, and drafts the rules (words never to use, copy and image rules, images to keep). The answer is applied without AI: sections marked in the page (sibling blocks grouped), line-length budgets, the M4 section map, all saved as the next version. You check it in the section editor (a strip under the top bar, a Rules tab, "Found on the source" on Look) and Confirm; only then can pages be built from it. Analyse again re-reads the page. Clone builds can't be published until B2.3 scores their leaks.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/analysis.py` (digest, blocks, apply, untag, caps, mock answer), `app/jobs/kinds/template_analyze.py`, `app/models/blueprint.py`, `app/templates.py` (clone editor state, `confirm_review`, `set_instructions`), `app/engines/template_outline.py` (clone checks), `app/clones.py` (`start_analysis`), `app/main.py` (`POST /templates/{id}/analyse`, `/review`, `/instructions`), `app/builds.py` (built only once confirmed), `app/publish/gate.py` (`residue_unscored`), `app/jobs/kinds/template_clone.py` (queues the analysis), registry, queue cap, config; tests `test_analysis.py`, `test_analysis_job.py`, fixture `funnelish_flat.html`. ### B2.3 — Clone-only build steps
- **Status:** complete (owner: go, with my recommendations; live check approved; not committed)
- **Date:** 2026-10-31
- **Summary:** a build from a confirmed clone now comes out as the brand's page. It reads the clone's analysis (the source's palette for recolouring, its logos for the swap, its names and site for the clean-up), sorts the source's images into slots (no image vision yet), and runs the six clone steps: strip the store's header, footer, chat and overlays; replace the source's names; drop its scripts and keep layout libraries and fonts by copying them in; point its links at the product; purge every foreign file; and score what's left. Clones are blocked from publishing below a leak score of 85 or with any source name left (both overridable). Clone builds list their own steps, and the Overview has a Source leaks card.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/clone_build.py` (hosts, source names, the steps, image sorting), `app/engines/residue.py` (the score), `app/fetch/copy_in.py` (copying fonts, libraries and kept images into assets), `app/pipeline/nodes/clone_only.py`, `load_inputs.py`, `swap_logos.py`, `prepare_media_slots.py`, `regenerate_variants.py`, `write_report.py`, `app/publish/gate.py`, `app/providers/storage.py` (`.js` copies); tests `test_clone_build.py` (13), `test_clone_pipeline.py` (1), fixture `tests/fixtures/clone/store_advertorial.html`. Web `lib/builds/steps.ts` (`CLONED_BUILD_STEPS`, `buildStepsFor`; + `steps.test.ts`), `components/features/builds/{build-report,build-steps,build-preview}.tsx` (Source leaks card, clone steps), `lib/validators/build.ts` (`residue`, `source`), `app/(app)/pages/[buildId]/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/dev/build-states/page.tsx` (state 20).
- **Schema changes:** none.
- **Decisions:** D100.
- **Deviations from spec:** the copying lives in `fetch/copy_in.py` (I/O beside the fetch ladder) rather than an engine; vendored scripts and fonts are stored files in the org's assets, not inlined (the inlined page stays an export format, D8); a store form becomes plain markup with its buttons wrapped in the product link; leftover third-party links that read like buttons go to the product, others keep their words without a link; a clone's option picker isn't rebuilt yet (warned); there's no wizard field for a custom button link, so links go to the product page (else the brand's site).
- **Verification:** worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (153 files); pytest: the new tests 14/14, the related suites 89/89 (pipeline, analysis job, publish, clone, clone job, page engines). The full mock clone build passes every clone node in order and scores 100. Web typecheck, lint, tokens, prettier, vitest 191/191 (+2). Source leaks card checked in light and dark on /dev/build-states. **Live check (owner's go):** the three Resilia pages cloned, analysed with real Claude and built with real Claude copy (strict) and placeholder images, in a throwaway workspace deleted after with its files: leak scores 100, 93 (its FDA disclaimer's "cure") and 100; copy rewritten 63 %, 92 % and 66 %; 9–10 images copied in on two pages, 17–32 fonts copied, 28–45 foreign scripts dropped; no source name anywhere in the pages' markup. Real spend: **$1.28** (three analyses and three builds). The live pages showed store and tracking inline scripts that load nothing by address (Shopify stubs, UET, EdgeTag, Elevar, UpPromote, Kaching, Funnelish globals) and CSS comments with addresses; the markers were widened and re-run on the saved pages: only the page's own component script stays and no foreign address is left. (The live builds ran from my script without the job loop's heartbeat, so the regular loop marked their jobs lost after 90 s while they finished; not a pipeline issue.)
- **Owner actions requested:** test a clone build end to end (see the report).
- **Pushed (owner's go, 2026-10-31):** the owner's first clone build, BRE-2, was claimed by Railway's job loop on 85790d5, so it ran the old pass-through clone steps (Resilia's images, links and tracker left in; no leak score, so publish was blocked). B2.3 was committed and pushed so both job loops run the same code. The rebuild (BRE-3, on a private job queue so only B2.3 code could run it) scored 100 but had no images: lxml nests `<img>` inside a preceding `<source>` (it doesn't know HTML5 empty elements), so removing the source's foreign `<source>` removed the image. Fix: `engines/html.parse` moves children out of empty elements, as a browser does; regression test added; pushed as a follow-up.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Follow-up (owner's go, 2026-10-31): fewer idle database connections.** The Supabase session pooler takes 15 clients in all, and it refused a query once ("max clients reached") while Railway's deploy briefly ran old and new containers beside the local `pnpm dev`. Every worker process (API and job loop, local and Railway) kept 2 connections open in the main pool and 1 in the checkpoint pool, plus any it had grown for ten minutes. Now the main pool keeps 1 and the checkpoint pool none (builds open it), and spare connections close after 60 s unused (`POOL_MAX_IDLE_SECONDS`; psycopg closes one per period). Files: `services/worker/app/db.py`, `app/pipeline/checkpoint.py`. Verification: ruff, ruff format, mypy; the settings checked in a live process (main 1–7, checkpoint 0–4, idle 60 s); worker pytest 315 passed in the full run, whose 21 failures were this machine losing the network for several minutes (the database host wouldn't resolve, then reads timed out); all 19 passed on their own afterwards, the tenant-isolation test once the outage's leftover test workspaces (three, with their test logins) were deleted on the owner's go. Pushed on the owner's go (2026-11-03).
- **Follow-up (owner, 2026-11-03): the icon rail on the owner's MacBook.** Collapsing showed tooltips beside every icon, and a sideways scroll bar sat under the brand marks. Tooltips: `RailTooltip` holds `open` false while the sidebar is wide, and Radix then never reports a close, so every row hovered past the delay stayed "open" and showed once collapsed; it now takes opens only in the rail and starts closed when the rail opens or closes. Scroll bar: the nav scrolled up and down (`overflow-y-auto`), which scrolls sideways too, and the headings keep their width in the rail (99 px in 44 px); it shows where scroll bars always show (a mouse attached, or macOS's "Show scroll bars: Always"), not in headless checks. The nav is now `overflow-x-hidden`. Files: `apps/web/components/app/sidebar-parts.tsx`, `sidebar.tsx`. Verification: web typecheck, lint, prettier, vitest (components/app 26/26); in Chromium with a throwaway account (deleted after), the old code showed the hovered row's tooltip after collapsing and could scroll sideways (overflow-x auto); the fixed code shows no tooltip after collapsing (twice, with rows hovered past the delay), one tooltip for the hovered icon in the rail, and overflow-x hidden.

### I3 — Live results in the brand fetch and clone checklists
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go ahead"; committed and pushed on the owner's go)
- **Date:** 2026-11-03
- **Summary:** while a brand is fetched or a page is cloned and analysed, each finished step in the checklist shows what it actually found, under its label: the brand's name, then its colours and fonts; the clone's builder and page title, then the analysis's block and image counts, the colours and brand words, and the number of sections. The worker adds `data.found` (a short line and up to four colours, normalised and capped in `app/jobs/found.py`) to the progress events those steps already sent; there are no new AI calls. `jobStepStates` validates it and hands it to the tracker, which draws it for any job that reports it. The idea is Stageshow's "results arrive" pattern; the kit itself stays out of real UI (D101), and the motion is CSS in the tracker at Emil timings (240 ms, 40 ms apart).

The tracker's strike-through now targets the label's own text (`.ui-progress__text`), not the label's last child, so a found line is never struck.

### B2.4a — Top 5 ranking: the competitors come in
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go ahead" with my recommendations; committed and pushed on the owner's go, 513c17b)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** a Top 5 ranking template now gets a Competitors step in the wizard (four product pages in rank order, #2 to #5, and neutral labels or real names). The build reads the four pages at once in a new first step, `pull_competitors`: each runs the product pull, its JSON-LD rating and reviews, and one AI pass for what the page claims; what was read is stored per build in the new `build_competitors` table and fills the ranking's competitor entries and table rows. Ranks close up over a page that couldn't be read; the build fails only if none can be read. Competitor entries are locked from the copy pass and any field without data is removed, never invented. The build page lists the competitors in a new Competitors card. A starter ranking shell (`top5-ranking`) is in the library until the owner's design replaces it.

### B2.4b — Top 5 ranking: the page goes out
- **Status:** complete (owner: yes to the four wording questions, "go" on the disclosure wording; committed and pushed on the owner's go, b960ea6)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** a ranking build gets a new step after reading the competitors, `compare_products`: one AI call (the copy model) writes "Our take" for each competitor read (What stands out, Worth knowing) and picks up to four points for the comparison table (live run: Upper material, Where made, Returns or exchanges, Comfort features), with a cell per product, #1 included, or "Not stated". It works only from facts with ids (#1 from the brand's product data, #2 to #5 from their pages), every line and cell cites what it rests on, and the page keeps those ids (`data-lp-sources`). The claims check traces the page (a cited line must cite real facts and its numbers must be in them; any other number in a competitor's entry or row must be on that competitor's page; the page must keep its Advertisement line and a disclosure), adds the ranking rules (is the best, top rated, beats every other, disparaging words; every superlative high risk), and stops flagging "our #1 pick" anywhere. The review desk re-runs the trace after every edit from the facts saved in the report. The Advertisement line and the two disclosures are filled with fixed wording, the brand and the date, and locked. The Competitors card shows each take and "Side by side". If the AI call fails twice, the page leaves out the takes and extra columns and the build goes on with a warning. The AI calls the top product "our pick", with a backstop that rewrites a stray "#1".
- **Key files:** worker `app/pipeline/nodes/compare_products.py` (new), `app/engines/comparison.py` (new: facts, tidy, fill, disclosures, trace, `check_claims`), `app/engines/compliance.py` and `compliance_rules.py` (ranking rules, "our #1"), `app/pipeline/nodes/compliance_scan.py`, `app/desk.py`, `app/pipeline/{graph.py,state.py}`, `nodes/write_report.py`, `app/models/competitor.py`; tests `tests/test_comparison.py` (new, 10), `test_ranking.py`, `test_ranking_pipeline.py`, `test_pipeline.py`; template `templates/library/top5-ranking/{index.html,instructions.json}`; web `lib/builds/steps.ts`, `lib/validators/build.ts`, `components/features/builds/build-report.tsx`, `/dev/build-states` (state 21, plus `#state-N` anchors), tests; docs D103, MILESTONES (B2.4c), DESIGN, BUILD_SPEC §8.7 note, template-onboarding markup rows. Also in this change: the neutral-naming fix from the owner's test (B2.4a follow-up, above).
- **Schema changes:** none (the comparison and its facts live in the build's report). **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D103.
- **Deviations from the plan:** none of substance. Comparison data is per build in the report, not `product_strategy` (recorded in D103 and the spec). The live run surfaced "for #1" in a take, flagged by the #1 rule; fixed with the prompt and the `our_pick` backstop (not re-run live).
- **Verification:** worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (164 files); the full pytest suite on the cloud dev database, 357 passed (including the comparison tests and the ranking build end to end: takes and cells cite facts, the disclosures name the brand, the trace is clean, no high-risk findings); after the last `our_pick` change, the comparison, ranking and end-to-end tests again (20 passed). Web typecheck, eslint, token check, Prettier; vitest 207/207 (a first run timed out in 6 unrelated files while the worker suite loaded the machine; all pass on a rerun). No schema change, so no pgTAP run.
- **Live run (owner's go, ~$0.07):** ALL-5 (same org, product, four links and settings as the owner's ALL-3), on a local job loop with its own queue and live Claude: read 4 of 4, "Compared 5 products on 4 points", cost compare_products $0.021, copy $0.018, competitor reads $0.030. Every take and cell cites facts; the only finding was the "#1" wording above. The page rendered at desktop and 390 px (the table scrolls inside its box; no page overflow), with the Advertisement line, the dated disclosure under the headline and the footer disclosure.
- **Note:** the end-to-end test re-seeds the starter template into the shared library, so the live library already has the new markup. Until this is pushed, a ranking build on the live site (old code) shows the template's placeholder "Our take" and a "Feature" column.
- **Owner actions requested:** push (the live library is ahead of the live code, see note); try a ranking build; bring in your ranking design for B2.4c.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B2.4c (the owner's ranking shell) is deferred to the later to-do list (owner, 2026-11-04); the next build is B3.1.
- **Follow-up (owner: "make sure all is checked and cleared", same day): code review and fixes.** A high-effort review of the whole change found, and I fixed: (1) on ranking pages the ranking rules and their high bar also ran over a competitor's own quoted claims and reviews, which are locked, so a competitor's "#1 best-selling" could block publishing with nothing the user could change: quoted competitor text now keeps the normal rules; (2) with real names, a number in a product's name ("Capri 2") used in a take was flagged: names are facts now; (3) the neutral backstop ran every competitor's names over every line, so a generic name ("Super Greens") could turn #1's cell into a competitor's label: it now touches only each competitor's own take and cells; (4) the "our #1" exemption also let "your #1" and "our #1 bestselling" through: it now covers only "our #1 pick / choice / recommendation / favourite"; (5) a number in a reviewer's name was flagged; (6) a take with two blank lines left an empty "Our take" box; (7) duplicate point names made a repeated column; plus tidy-ups (one truncation helper, one ranking type constant in `app/models/template.py`, one competitor-names helper `competitors.own_names`, the facts computed once in `compare_products` and kept in state, one page parse for both checks). The copy rules for every ranking template now say "our top pick", never a superlative as fact. Also fixed (outside B2.4b, harmless log noise seen in the live run): the browser fetch now clears its request routes before closing, so the job loop no longer logs cancelled-route tracebacks. The starter's library thumbnail was re-rendered (the test seeds without thumbnails, so a later seed saw no change). Live check ALL-6 (same four links, ~$0.07): read 4 of 4, compared on 4 points, "Checked the claims: nothing flagged", takes say "our pick", no competitor names on the page. My test step 3 in the report (edit a competitor's line on the desk) was wrong: competitor entries are locked there; corrected in the report. Verified after the fixes: worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (164 files); the full pytest suite, 363 passed (7 new tests for the fixes); after the browser fix, the comparison, ranking, end-to-end and fetch tests again (43 passed); web typecheck, eslint, token check, Prettier, vitest 207/207.


### B3.1a — The page editor
- **Status:** complete (owner: yes to the split, the template editor's look, autosave with one undo step per line and the Edit page button; "go"; committed locally with B3.1b, not pushed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** a built page opens full screen at `/pages/[id]/editor` from **Edit page** on the build page. Left: the selected section (move up and down, duplicate, remove, by the template's rules; Improve with AI) over the page's sections (drag to reorder). Middle: the page, Desktop or Phone; click any line and type: it saves a second after you stop and when you leave the line, through the desk's copy save, and one line's saves share one undo step; lines filled from data say so; text filled from data inside a line can't be typed over; Enter or a click away ends the line, Escape puts it back. Right: Ask AI, a box that sends a note to the section (or page) redraft, shows it working and lists recent changes with Undo on the newest; it hides from its header (remembered per browser). Top bar: back to the review desk, save status, Undo and Redo (⌘Z, ⇧⌘Z, also from inside the page). Redo is new: Undo keeps the page it undid until the next new change. Undo, Redo, section changes and AI requests first save the line being typed and go after it.
- **Key files:** worker `app/desk.py` (`edit_sections`, `redo`, Undo keeping a redo row, typing merges in `_change`, `_layout` with the template's rules and container groups), `app/models/desk.py` (`DeskSection`, `layout`, `can_redo`, `redo_reason`, `typing`, `SectionRequest`), `app/main.py` (`/sections`, `/redo`), `tests/test_desk.py` (+3); migration `supabase/migrations/20261104111127_page_editor_snapshots.sql`; web `app/(editor)/pages/[buildId]/editor/page.tsx`, `components/features/page-editor/{page-editor,section-panel,page-canvas,composer-panel,page-editor-skeleton}.tsx` + test, `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts` (`?mode=write`, the write bridge), `components/features/builds/desk-context.tsx` (redo, section changes, quiet typing saves, the canvas's frame version, changes on the newest version), `lib/pages/editor.ts` + test, `lib/validators/desk.ts`, `app/(app)/pages/[buildId]/page.tsx` (Edit page), `components/app/{icon-action,shortcut-button}.tsx` (shared with the template editor), `components/ui/icons.ts` (+3); `types/database.ts` regenerated.
- **Schema changes:** `build_snapshots.merge_key` and `build_snapshots.redo` (applied to the cloud dev project with `supabase db push`; the live code ignores them).
- **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D104.
- **Deviations from the plan:** the left panel is 360 px (the template editor's), not 320, so long section names fit; section rows show their position ("01") rather than a role. Found in the browser check and fixed: Undo pressed while a typing save was still on its way was refused as "changed elsewhere" (saves take 5 to 7 s on this Mac); now the editor saves the line first and every change uses the newest version.
- **Verification:** worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (164 files); the full pytest suite on the cloud dev database, 366 passed (3 new: section changes by the template's rules, typing merges into one undo step, Redo until a new change); pgTAP passes. Web typecheck, eslint, token check, Prettier; vitest 212/212 (page editor components, editor rules). The migration is applied to the cloud dev project and the types regenerated. Browser check against the local web and worker with a throwaway account and a copy of a ranking page (deleted afterwards): Edit page opens the editor; typing in the headline saved twice as one undo step without reloading the page; a locked line says why; typing then Undo at once saved the line first, then undid it with no conflict; Redo put it back; moving the comparison table up saved and reloaded the page; light and dark screenshots checked. Not checked in the browser: sending Ask AI (it would have run a live redraft on the shared queue; the redraft itself is tested and unchanged).
- **Owner actions requested:** open a built page → Edit page; type in a line, move a section, Undo/Redo; try Ask AI on a section (a redraft: about 1 to 2 cents with live Claude).

### B3.1b — Lander: the editor around a floating composer
- **Status:** complete (owner: yes to the split, the Sections drawer, "Work with Lander", the sparkle, keep the mic, Sidekick's timings with DialKit, the Mover on the Sections panel; "go ahead and build, build with intention"; committed locally with B3.1a on the owner's go, not pushed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** the page editor is reorganised page first, after Shopify's Sidekick (studied live, `docs/reference/sidekick-study.md`). The page fills one white panel under the top bar (back, name, save status, Sections, Desktop / Phone, Undo, Redo); Sections open as a drawer on its left (closed by default, resizable, remembered). The assistant is **Lander**: a "Work with Lander" pill floats at the foot of the page (mark, field, mic or send, Open Lander ⌘J). Sending or opening brings in Lander's panel, a second white panel that squeezes the page (resizable 300 to 450 px; over the page below 1100 px). Each request runs the existing redraft on its target (the selected section or the whole page) with the request as the note; Lander's reply follows the job: a status line, the plan, the steps, a change card (before and after, Show on page, Ask Lander to edit, Undo / Redo on the newest), a closing line. While Lander rewrites, those lines pulse on the page; a finished change flashes its lines and scrolls to them. Dictation through the browser's speech recognition. The motion uses Sidekick's measured timings, tunable on DialKit in development. Reloads now load behind the page shown and swap in when ready, so the canvas never blanks, and each change reloads once.
- **Key files:** web `app/(studio)/layout.tsx` and `app/(studio)/pages/[buildId]/editor/page.tsx` (moved from `(editor)`; `quietRedrafts`), `components/features/page-editor/{page-editor,editor-top-bar,page-canvas,page-editor-skeleton,lander-motion}.tsx`, `components/features/page-editor/lander/{floating-composer,lander-panel,lander-thread,change-card,lander-composer,composer-parts,lander-mark,stream-text,rolling-label,use-lander,use-dictation}.ts(x)` + `lander.test.tsx`, `lib/pages/lander.ts` + test, `components/features/builds/desk-context.tsx` (`startRedraft`, `settledRedrafts`, `quietRedrafts`), `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts` (the write bridge's `lp-busy`, `lp-flash`, `lp-placed`), `app/globals.css` (`--spacing-lander-bar`, `--spacing-lander-panel`, the Lander-Aura tokens and the `lander-aura` ring), `components/ui/icons.ts` (+13); `composer-panel.tsx` removed; docs D105, DESIGN (the editor row), MILESTONES (B3.1b, B3.1c), CLAUDE.md §4 (the route), `docs/reference/sidekick-study.md`.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none (DialKit was already in).
- **Decisions:** D105 (and a note on D104).
- **Deviations from the plan:** B3.1b keeps today's redraft as Lander's only action (as agreed), so a request rewrites the target's words; it can't yet move sections or change images (B3.1c). No stop button (there is no job cancel yet). The conversation lasts for the visit. Found in the browser checks and fixed: the DialKit panel opened over the editor (now closed by default); suggestion rows and the change card's title truncated badly (now wrap, and the title truncates before "· N lines"); a queued request said "Thinking…" for seconds (now "Waiting for a worker…", as the review desk says); the canvas went blank for one to two seconds after every Undo, Redo or change (now loads behind and swaps once scrolled to your place, with a glint along the canvas top and "Updating the page…" in the save status meanwhile; a finished change's flash waits for the page it made); and every change reloaded the page twice, because the frame's address carried the build's `updated_at`, which every edit moves a moment later (now taken as the editor opens).
- **Verification:** web typecheck, eslint (React Compiler rules), token check, Prettier; vitest 223/223 (Lander's thread rules, the composer and change card, the section panel). Worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (164 files); the full pytest suite on the cloud dev database, 366 passed (no worker changes in B3.1b; run to confirm nothing moved). Browser check against the local servers with a throwaway account and a copy of ALL-6, deleted afterwards (`.e2e-logs/lander/check.py`; one live redraft per run, about 1 cent, three runs): the editor opens with the page full width and the pill; opening and closing recorded frame by frame and compared with Sidekick (the page starts squeezing about 130 ms after the click and settles in about 490 ms; the panel fades in over 1.6 s from 200 ms; it closes in about 0.15 s while the page widens in about 0.45 s); Sections drawer; a suggestion sent: working (Rewriting 6 lines, the card's skeleton, the lines pulsing on the page), done (Completed 3 steps, the card with 6 lines, the closing line); Show on page flashes the lines; the card's Undo shows "undone" with Redo and the page reverts; Redo brings it back; light and dark screenshots. A second script (`.e2e-logs/lander/probe.py`, no paid calls) typed a change and timed Undo and Redo: the shown page never blanked, each change loaded once, and the status went Saving… (about 4.5 s: the desk call to the cloud database from this Mac), Updating the page… (about 2 s: the dev server loading the page), Saved · version N. Not checked: dictation (needs a microphone; the mic and its states are covered by the component), the overlay layout below 1100 px in the browser.
- **Owner actions requested:** open a built page → Edit page; try Work with Lander (a request runs a redraft: about 1 to 2 cents with live Claude), Show on page, Undo / Redo on the card, Sections, the mic in Chrome; tune the motion on the DialKit panel (bottom left, development only) and paste the values if you change them.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B3.1c — Lander as an agent with the editor's tools (lines, sections, images, colours), image attachments, stop, saved conversations.
- **Follow-up (owner, same day): the page keeps its room.** Opening Lander puts the section list away (only the page and Lander's panel show), and closing Lander brings it back if it was open; Sections pressed while Lander is open shows both. The list is narrower: 300 px by default (Shopify's theme-editor size, from memory, not measured), 260 to 420 with the Mover; widths remembered from the wider list start over (`lp-page-editor-sections-width-v2`). At 1512 px wide the page gets 1198 px with the list, 1136 with Lander, 836 with both. Verified: web typecheck and eslint; in the browser (throwaway account, no paid calls, `.e2e-logs/lander/layout_check.py`): list open, Lander opened (list away), Sections pressed (both), Lander closed (list stays), reopened (away), closed (back).
- **Follow-up 2 (owner, same day): no sideways swiping.** Measured at 1512, 1440, 1280 and 1100 px wide in every state: the editor itself never overflowed; the swipe was inside the page, which reflowed into a tablet layout as the canvas narrowed (the ranking's comparison table scrolls sideways below about 660 px). Desktop now lays the page out at the canvas width but never narrower than 1280 px and zooms it out to fit (`DESKTOP_WIDTH` in `page-canvas.tsx`; the zoom shows beside Desktop / Phone); Phone stays 390 px. The page box is out of the layout flow (a first try with it in the flow widened the editor to 1806 px in a 1512 px window; caught by the same measurement). At 1512 px: 100 % closed, 89 % with Sections, 84 % with Lander, 61 % with both.
