Lander's panel lost its Mover: 356 px, fixed. The composer floats over the thread's foot and the messages fade out over an eased 24 px just above it as they scroll under (`lander-thread-fade`, a mask after Stageshow's edge fades). Suggestions now offer only sections with lines Lander can rewrite (the first suggestion had offered a section filled entirely from data, which Lander then refused; found by the browser check). **The markup guard (#3, the owner's go):** `copy_rules.protect` drops any rewritten line carrying markup written as text that the original didn't have (`</br>`, `<b>`, `&lt;br&gt;`; "<3" isn't markup), so the original line stays; it covers the build's copy pass and redrafts. Verified: web typecheck, eslint, token check, vitest; worker ruff, ruff format, mypy, the copy and redraft tests (23 passed, 1 new); a full suite run reached two-thirds with no failures before a session restart cut it off, then the copy, redraft, pipeline, ranking-pipeline and desk tests again (50 passed); in the browser (throwaway account, two live redrafts, about 2 cents; `.e2e-logs/lander/debug_check.py`): the bar 6 px above the foot with the purple ring in light and dark, no resize handle on the panel, the suggestion runs, and the closing line fading under the composer in light and dark. A first run hit a 401 from the worker on the brand-new account's first request (a fresh-token timing hiccup, not this change); the rerun passed.

### B3.1c — Lander becomes an agent
- **Status:** complete (owner: yes to the four questions (split with B3.1d, one request one undo step, Sonnet 5.5, every request through the agent); "my tests should run on actual api usage"; "go"; committed and pushed on the owner's go, with B3.1a and B3.1b)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** a request to Lander is now one `agent_turn` job in which Claude (Sonnet 5.5) works on a copy of the page with tools, up to 8 rounds: read the page, edit lines, move / duplicate / remove sections, recolour, check wording against the claims rules. The result is saved as one desk change, so Undo takes a whole request back. The thread shows Lander's own plan, each step as it happens, a card per change (lines before and after; one-line cards for section moves and recolours), one Undo / Redo under the request's changes, and its closing line. Stop (the send disc becomes a Stop disc while it works) keeps what was done by the next step. The conversation is kept per page and comes back after a reload; New chat starts another. Lander's requests are about the whole page until you pick a section; requests take up to 500 characters. Also: the editor's top bar caps the page's name (full name in a tooltip), and the dev-only Next badge is off (it covered the send and Stop buttons).
- **Key files:** worker `app/agent.py` (tools, prompt, conversations, Stop), `app/jobs/kinds/agent_turn.py` (the loop), `app/models/agent.py`, `app/jobs/registry.py`, `app/main.py` (`/builds/{id}/agent`, `GET` the conversation, `/agent/stop`), `app/desk.py` (shared `copy_change`, `section_change`, `recolor_change`; Lander's job counts as the page's job), `app/providers/anthropic.py` (tools, prompt caching, reply blocks, scripted mocks), `app/engines/copy_rules.py` (`brief_parts`), `app/pipeline/nodes/load_inputs.py` (`template_html=False`), `tests/test_agent.py` (10); migration `supabase/migrations/20261104202455_lander_agent.sql`; web `lib/validators/agent.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts` (`startAgent`, `getAgentThread`, `stopAgent`), `app/api/builds/[buildId]/agent/route.ts` and `agent/stop/route.ts`, `components/features/builds/desk-context.tsx` (`startAgent`, `stopAgent`), `lib/pages/lander.ts` + test (events to steps, status, saved conversations), `components/features/page-editor/lander/{use-lander.ts,lander-thread.tsx,change-card.tsx,composer-parts.tsx,lander-composer.tsx}` + test, `page-editor.tsx`, `editor-top-bar.tsx`, `lib/validators/desk.ts` and `lib/builds/desk.ts` (the "agent" scope), `redraft-controls.tsx` ("Lander is working on…"), `components/ui/icons.ts` (+3), `next.config.ts`, `types/database.ts` (regenerated); docs D106, BUILD_SPEC §11.3 note, MILESTONES (B3.1c, B3.1d), DESIGN (the editor row).
- **Schema changes:** `agent_messages.thread_id`, `agent_messages.job_id`, writes revoked from members (the worker writes); `jobs.cancel_requested_at`. Applied to the cloud dev project with `supabase db push`; types regenerated.
- **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D106.
- **Deviations from the plan:** one job per request runs the whole loop (BUILD_SPEC §11.3 said each turn a job), recorded in D106 and the spec. Stop waits for a Claude call already under way (its tool calls aren't run). Found in the live check and fixed: the composer had targeted the first section (the disclosure strip) by default and Lander's first step read "Read 0 lines in Publication and disclosure"; requests are now about the whole page until you pick a section, and the step reads "Read the page: 120 lines in 10 sections". The Next dev badge covered Stop (the check couldn't click it); turned off.
- **Verification:** worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (168 files); `tests/test_agent.py` 10 passed (the tools on a page; a request through the API and the job; Stop keeping what was done; a failure saving nothing; follow-ups seeing the conversation), the desk, redraft and copy tests after the refactor (40 passed), and a full suite run (two-thirds through with no failures when pushed; the owner is told the final count). Web typecheck, eslint, token check, Prettier, vitest 223/223. Live browser checks against the local servers with live Claude (throwaway accounts and a copy of ALL-6, deleted afterwards; `.e2e-logs/lander/agent_check.py`, `stop_check.py`; about 10–15 cents, one request measured at 2.2 cents): "Make the headline punchier, and move the comparison table up" → plan, "Rewrote 1 line in Headline and how we ranked", "Moved Comparison table up", saved, closing, 20 s, and the page showed both; a follow-up in the same conversation ("make the verdict warmer", 18 s); Undo on it, then Redo offered; a reload brought the conversation back; Stop 9 s into a long request → "Stopped after 1 step", "Stopped. Nothing changed."; light and dark screenshots.
- **Owner actions requested:** open a built page → Edit page → Lander; try a request that needs two kinds of change, a follow-up, Undo under a reply, a reload, and Stop during a long request (each request about 2 to 6 cents with live Claude).
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B3.1d — image attachments as context, Recents, thumbs on replies.
- **Follow-up (owner, same day): the thread follows new replies.** With the thread full and the reader scrolled so messages sat behind the composer, a new request didn't bring the conversation down, and the thread's own scrolling could switch following off. Now only the reader scrolling up (wheel, touch, keys) stops it, coming back near the bottom starts it again, and a new request always scrolls down (smoothly; at once when a conversation loads). Verified: web typecheck, eslint, vitest; in the browser (throwaway account; a long saved conversation written to the database; one live request, about 3 cents; `.e2e-logs/lander/follow_check.py`): loaded 0 px from the bottom, scrolled up 900 px, sent a request: 0 px from the bottom every second until Lander's reply finished, its closing line clear of the composer.
- **Follow-up 2 (same day): the full worker suite.** 373 passed, 4 failed, all in `tests/test_providers.py`: their fake API replies are plain objects without the SDK's `model_dump`, which the provider now called on every reply block (real SDK replies always have it, so the pushed code was fine live). The provider now turns any block into a dict (`_block_dict`). Verified: ruff, mypy; `tests/test_providers.py` and `tests/test_agent.py` 28 passed. Committed locally, to go out with the next push (pushing would restart the live worker during the owner's test).

Lander's tool description had wrongly said "primary (buttons, links)". Now `recolor.color_roles` reads which colour the page's buttons, links and headings use and read_page says so ("On this page: buttons use the accent colour"); on a page with colour variables Lander's recolour sets only the variables (`recolor(..., literals=False)`: no sweep, no literal rewrites); the tool's reply lists each colour before and after and what uses it, and the step names it ("Changed the accent colour (buttons)"); and Lander is told to re-read the page when asked whether a change took effect. The owner's page keeps the earlier recolour until Undo (newest change). (2) Thin, quiet scrollbars app-wide and in the page preview (after Shopify's admin). (3) Header titles capped on every page and both editors, with the full title in a tooltip only when cut (`components/app/truncated-title.tsx`). (4) Lander's "Working on it" status uses the @dotmatrix dotm-3x3-11 loader at 12 px in the text colour (`pnpm dlx shadcn@latest add @dotmatrix/dotm-3x3-11`; vendored in `components/ui/dotmatrix/` with an LP-CHANGES note, kept out of ESLint and Prettier like Stageshow; wrapped by `components/app/working-dots.tsx`). Verified: worker ruff, mypy, the agent, redraft, pipeline and page-engine tests (74 passed, 1 new: the owner's button case); web typecheck, eslint, token check, Prettier, vitest 223/223; in the browser (throwaway accounts; scrollbars shown): the review desk's long title cut at 256 px with its tooltip, no tooltip on "Pages", 10 px scrollbar lanes in Lander's thread and the page preview, the loader 12 × 12 px in light and dark (one live request, about 2 cents).

### B3.1d — Lander styles what it sees
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go and commit and push"; committed and pushed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-04
- **Summary:** Lander can now restyle what's on the page and check its work by looking. Three new tools: **inspect** (the element around a line and the boxes it sits in, up to its section, each with the CSS that styles it), **style** (safe CSS on one box: spacing, size, layout of its content, type, colours, borders), and **look** (renders the page with Lander's changes in the guarded browser and shows Claude a picture of the box around a line or a section, at desktop or phone width; the picture shows under the step in the thread). Requests can carry up to three **reference pictures** (attach, paste or drop; sized down in the browser; thumbnails in the request). A style change is a card ("Fitted the byline box to its text with even padding", properties under it); every request is still one undo step. ### B3.2a — The image studio, without AI
- **Status:** complete (owner: "1. yes. 2. yes (although, i don't have fal setup yet, but we can build for that, and move to the next build till fal is in)"; "commit and push"; committed and pushed)

### B3.2b — AI pictures in the image studio

In the browser on a copy of ALL-1 (one live Claude request, about 3 cents), filmed: "Reading the page…" at 8.1 s, "Thinking…" 9.3 s, the plan 15.0 s, the second step 16.2 s, its card 16.6 s, the closing 29.4 s, done 29.6 s; no rows overlapping; the composer's fade when scrolled.

The Supabase session pooler allows 15 connections in total and the local and Railway workers, the API and tests share them (EMAXCONNSESSION seen today). Recommended: the transaction pooler (port 6543) for the worker, or fewer connections per process; owner's call.

### The worker on Supabase's transaction pooler (D113)
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go"; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Summary:** The worker (API, job loop, tests) connects through Supabase's **transaction pooler** (port 6543) instead of the session pooler (5432). The session pooler gave each client its own server connection, 15 in all, shared by every process (local and Railway, API and job loop, tests); each process can open up to 11, so busy moments waited for a connection or failed (EMAXCONNSESSION, seen today). The transaction pooler shares the server connections between clients. Both connection pools turn prepared statements off (the pooler can hand each transaction to a different server connection); the LangGraph checkpointer keeps its `search_path=langgraph` option, which the pooler honours. Going back is only the port.
- **Key files:** worker `app/db.py`, `app/pipeline/checkpoint.py`, `.env.example`; local `services/worker/.env` (gitignored; port only); docs D113 (amends D73), DEPENDENCIES, CLAUDE.md §5.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D113.
- **Deviations from the plan:** none. Finding: it doesn't make a quiet request faster. Timed in mock mode (free) on a temporary copy of ALL-1, four requests each: send 3.4 s, picked up 1.4 s, first words 2.5 s, done 13.4 s on 5432; 3.4 s, 1.6 s, 2.6 s, 13.3 s on 6543. Its gain is no waiting and no connection errors when several processes are busy. Most of the local time is the round trips from this machine to the database in Ireland; Railway sits about 20 ms from it.
- **Verification:** a read-only probe through 6543 (plain and repeated queries without prepared statements, the checkpointer's search_path across statements, a checkpoint read); worker ruff, ruff format, mypy (185 files); the full worker suite on 6543, 413 passed (38 min; includes the pgTAP tenant-isolation tests and the build pipeline with its checkpoints); after the follow-ups, the Lander and fetch tests again on 6543 and web vitest 233/233. The temporary copy and its jobs and files were deleted afterwards.
- **Owner actions requested:** after this is pushed and Railway has deployed it, change `DATABASE_URL` on both Railway services (API and job loop) from port `5432` to `6543`.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Follow-up (owner, same day: "look at the last conversation log"):** "is the phone layout good?" on ALL-1 failed twice ("Lander couldn't finish that"). Lander was looking at each section on a phone; the **sticky shop bar** is hidden (`display: none`) until the visitor scrolls, the screenshot waited 5 s for it to show, and that error failed the whole request. A part that isn't showing (hidden, or a fixed bar parked off the screen) is now a step, "Couldn't look at Sticky shop bar (phone): it isn't showing", and Lander is told why and carries on (the owner's next try already came back done). Files: worker `app/fetch/browser.py` (`NotShownError`), `app/agent.py` (`PartNotShown`), `app/jobs/kinds/agent_turn.py`, tests in `test_agent.py` and `test_fetch.py` (real Chromium: a hidden bar and a parked one).
- **Follow-up (owner, same day):** Lander's findings and options are **numbered points**: its instructions now ask for a numbered list with a few bold words leading each item when it reports several findings or offers several fixes (they used to forbid lists, so points came as "The first thing… The second thing…"), and a number in the user's reply means that item. The chat draws "1. " lines with the number in a white chip (thin ring, light shadow; owner: "i don't want the purple") and bullets as a dot, each with its text hanging beside it. Lander's last ALL-1 reply was rewritten as points 1 to 4 at the owner's request (the original is kept in the session's scratchpad). Files: web `components/features/page-editor/lander/stream-text.tsx`, `lander.test.tsx`; worker `app/agent.py`; DESIGN.
- **Follow-up (owner, same day):** many change cards **stack** so a long request doesn't fill the thread: four or more one-line cards in a row (restyles, moves, recolours, image changes) show as the newest card with the edges of two more under it and the total in a white chip on its top-right corner, rolling up as cards arrive; clicking unfolds them all and the chip stacks them again. ### B3.1f — Lander does big changes in one go
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go"; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** A Lander request now runs within a **budget**, 30 rounds, $1 and 5 minutes (whichever first), instead of stopping after 8 rounds; it's told when a quarter is left so it finishes the most important changes. If it still runs out, what it did is saved as usual and the reply ends with **Keep going**, which carries on in the same conversation. Its styling tool can set the same CSS on **every element alike** in one call (all the item numbers, every section's box) and can style **the page itself** (its own background behind and between the sections). Its instructions ask for few rounds: several actions per reply, alike elements together, one look per section after a batch.
- **Key files:** worker `app/agent.py` (`Budget`, `style_element`'s `all_like`, the page as a target, the instructions), `app/engines/styling.py` (`alike`, `apply_alike`, `PAGE`), `app/jobs/kinds/agent_turn.py` (the budget loop, `more`), `app/config.py` (`LP_AGENT_MAX_ROUNDS`, `LP_AGENT_MAX_USD`, `LP_AGENT_MAX_SECONDS`), tests in `test_agent.py` (3 new) and `test_styling.py` (2 new); web `lib/validators/agent.ts` (`more`), `components/features/page-editor/lander/use-lander.ts`, `lander-thread.tsx` (Keep going), `page-editor.tsx`, `lander.test.tsx` (1 new); docs D114, BUILD_SPEC §11.3, MILESTONES, DESIGN, DEPENDENCIES.

### B3.5 — Batch build
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go" to the plan and its recommendations; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** One product, a page from every template at once. The create wizard's Template step has **Build with every template**: on, every template card becomes a tick box, all ticked (untick any), and the hint counts them with the time it'll take; the later steps apply to every page, and a Top 5 ranking in the batch uses the Competitors step's one set of competitors. Review & build lists the templates with the cost (Claude about $0.05–0.35 a page; AI pictures free until fal, then $0.15 each) and the button reads "Build 4 pages". The worker creates **all the builds and their jobs in one transaction, or none**: every template, the product and the hourly cap are checked first, and past the cap it says how many still fit. Then the **batch screen** (`/pages/batch/<id>`) follows them: each page waiting its turn ("Waiting, 2nd in line": a workspace builds two at a time), the step it's on, ready to review, or failed with why and Try again, refreshing every 3 s, over a bar of how many are ready.
- **Key files:** worker `app/builds.py` (`create_batch`, `batch_state`, the shared `_template` / `_product` / `_add_build`), `app/jobs/queue.py` (`remaining`; jobs stamped with `clock_timestamp()`), `app/main.py` (`POST /builds/batch`, `GET /builds/batch/{id}`), `app/models/build.py`, `tests/test_batch.py` (new, 3); web `components/features/wizard/steps.tsx` (the switch, tick boxes), `create-wizard.tsx`, `review-step.tsx`, `lib/wizard/state.ts` (`batch`, `templates`, `batchPayload`), `lib/builds/actions.ts` (`createBatch`), `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/validators/build.ts`, `app/api/builds/batch/[batchId]/route.ts`, `app/(app)/pages/batch/[batchId]/page.tsx` and `components/features/builds/batch-view.tsx` (new) with tests; `supabase/migrations/20261106070409_build_batches.sql`; `types/database.ts` regenerated; docs D115, BUILD_SPEC §9, MILESTONES, DESIGN.
- **Schema changes:** `builds.batch_id uuid` (nullable) and an index on `(org_id, batch_id)`; pushed to the cloud dev project.

### B3.2c — Match product everywhere
- **Status:** complete (owner: answers to the plan, 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** Pictures that show a product that isn't the real one get recreated with the real product. The **check is automatic**: one Claude vision call (about 2 cents) sees up to three product photos and a thumbnail of each candidate picture and says which to fix; every build ends with it, and the image studio runs it again when the page's pictures have changed. **Match product** in the studio (one click, with its price) or Buddy's `match_product` fixes what it found: up to 10 pictures, each an edit on fal with one product photo as the reference, placed as **one undoable change**, each picture also a new version. Free placeholders and pictures that won't download are skipped without asking; generic pictures stay generic. In builds the fixes run only once pictures are live and the wizard chose AI pictures. Mock mode until there's a fal key (D83).

### B3.1g — Formatting selected text on the page
- **Status:** complete (owner: answers to the plan, 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06

Buddy can do the same with `format_text`. The toolbar copies the Buddy bar's look, with a stronger edge.

### B3.6 — The client review link
- **Status:** complete (owner: "your recommendation", migration push permitted, 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** Approving a page now keeps a copy of it as approved. On the review desk, **Client link** (after Approve / Back to review) makes a link and copies it as it's made. Anyone with the link sees the approved version, on desktop and phone, without signing in. The card shows the link with Copy, which version it shows, when it expires (30 days), how often it's been opened, and Turn off (asks first). One link per page: approving again moves the same link to the new version, and edits don't reach it until then (the card says so). The client's page at `/review/<token>` shows the page's own title and approval date, Desktop / Phone, and the page with its scripts off. It carries none of our branding, is kept out of search engines and doesn't pass its address on. An expired or turned-off link says it isn't available any more. The token isn't stored: it's signed with the worker's `LP_SHARE_SECRET`. Links are built on the production domain (`lp-platform-tan.vercel.app`), which Vercel's Standard Protection leaves public.
- **Key files:** `supabase/migrations/20261106181114_share_links.sql` (new; pushed to the cloud with the owner's permission), `supabase/tests/database/tenant_isolation.test.sql` (36 checks); worker `app/share.py` (new: tokens, the approved copy, make / read / turn off, opening a link), `app/models/share.py` (new), `app/desk.py` (Approve keeps the copy), `app/main.py` (`GET/POST/DELETE /builds/{id}/share`, public `GET /review/{token}` and `/html`), `app/config.py` (`lp_share_secret`), `tests/test_share.py` (new, 5), `tests/conftest.py` (the page fixture removes its whole folder); web `app/review/[token]/page.tsx` and `html/route.ts` (new), `components/features/review/review-view.tsx` (new: the page, its loading state, gone), `components/features/builds/client-link.tsx` (new) and its test (3), `app/api/builds/[buildId]/share/route.ts` (new), `lib/validators/share.ts`, `lib/queries/share.ts` (new), `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/supabase/proxy.ts` (`/review` public), `components/app/viewport-switch.tsx` (moved from the canvas, shared), `app/(app)/pages/[buildId]/page.tsx`; docs D119, BUILD_SPEC §11.4, DESIGN (desk header, `/review/[token]`), MILESTONES, DEPENDENCIES, CLAUDE.md (layout; Vercel protection and the production domain), `.env.example`.
- **Schema changes:** `share_links` (members read their org's rows; one live link per page); `builds.approved_copy_path`, `approved_copy_version`, `approved_copy_at`. Types regenerated. **New dependencies:** none. **New env var:** `LP_SHARE_SECRET` on the worker (set locally; Railway needs it).
- **Decisions:** D119.
- **Deviations from the spec:** the link shows the page's approved copy, not an undo snapshot (those get removed); no `token_hash` (the token is signed, not stored, so Copy works any time); a visit counts when the page loads (the title is asked for more than once in development).
- **Verification:** `pnpm typecheck` (tsc, mypy 195 files), `pnpm lint` (eslint, token checks, Prettier, ruff), vitest 252 passed (51 files); worker share, desk, formatting, product-match, Buddy and image studio tests 81 passed; pgTAP tenant isolation passed (36). In the browser on a temporary copy (removed), light and dark: Approve, then Client link → Make link (copied to the clipboard); the client's page signed out returned 200 with the page's title, `noindex, nofollow`, the page in its frame, Phone at 390 px; the team's card then said "Opened once"; Turn off → the link said "This link isn't available any more". The console was clean apart from the page's own scripts being blocked, which is intended. The loading state measured box for box against the loaded bar at 1440 and 1024 px. Not checked: the "can't be shown right now" state (worker down), and the live site (needs the push and the Railway secret).

### B4.1 — The Shopify editor map
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go", 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** Each template now has a **Shopify editor map**, the plan M4 will publish from. It says how the page divides into Shopify sections (one per marked section, named from its label in 25 characters or fewer) and which texts and pictures become blocks people can edit in Shopify's theme editor, each with a role ("Headline", "Price", "Button", "Product picture"…). It's worked out from the markers our templates already carry, with **no AI**. A **dry run** works on any page (template or build) and checks it against Shopify's limits: 24 sections, 600 blocks a section, names, file name lengths. It also reports what stays as it is (outside every section, inside forms or embedded media) and a page with no sections at all. Maps are saved for every template (library and workspaces; 6 of 7 ready, the 7th a never-analysed test clone). New ones get theirs when seeded, copied or analysed. The template editor's **Details** tab shows **Shopify editing**: the totals, any issues, and each section's editable texts and pictures. The B4.2 route without the paid Shopify app (Shopify's free Theme Access password with Shopify CLI) is recorded as D121, proposed.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/editor_map.py` (new: derive, effective, plan, report, file names), `app/models/editor_map.py` (new), `app/cli/editor_map.py` (new), `app/templates.py` (the card's report; the map copied with a library template), `app/models/template.py` (`TemplateManifest.editor_map`, `TemplateEditorState.shopify`), `app/cli/seed_library.py`, `app/jobs/kinds/template_analyze.py`, `tests/test_editor_map.py` (new, 13), `tests/test_template_editor.py`; web `components/features/templates/editor/shopify-card.tsx` (new) and its test (2), `details-panel.tsx`, `lib/validators/template.ts`; docs D120, D121 (proposed), BUILD_SPEC §12.6, MILESTONES, DESIGN, CLAUDE.md §5, the template-onboarding skill (`data-lp-static`, `editor_map` in template.json).

### B4.2 — Editable pages in Shopify
- **Status:** complete (owner: the Theme Access password as the go, the migration push permitted, 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** A page can go to a brand's Shopify store as **editable sections**: every text, picture and video the editor map marks can be changed in Shopify's own theme editor, without a paid Shopify app.
  - **The password:** the store's free **Theme Access** password, added in Settings → Connections (checked with Shopify, kept in Vault), lets LP write the theme files through Shopify CLI.
  - **Publishing:** the published modal's Shopify row offers **Editable sections** or **One block** (as before). Each editable publish:
    - moves the pictures to Shopify Files;
    - builds the theme files from the page and its map: shared block types, one section per map section with **static blocks** at each editable spot, a bare layout, the CSS and the template;
    - checks Shopify's sizes, then uploads in two steps, block types first, only adding LP's own `lp-` files;
    - makes a new hidden draft page `<key>-v<N>`.
  - **Edit in Shopify** opens the theme editor on the draft's template; Shopify's editor can't open a hidden page.
  - **Tested on FourU** with the owner's BRĒZ page: 15 sections, 113 texts and 18 pictures, published in 27 seconds through the app; the owner opened it in the theme editor on their M5.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/shopify_theme.py` (new), `app/providers/shopify_cli.py` (new), `app/jobs/kinds/publish_shopify_editable.py` (new), `app/engines/editor_map.py` (`walk` keeps each block's element; 25 sections; no block limit for static blocks; a picture inside a text belongs to it), `app/publishing.py` (the password; the editable destination), `app/providers/shopify.py` (`page_exists`), `app/models/publishing.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/jobs/registry.py`, `app/config.py` (`lp_shopify_cli`), `Dockerfile` (Node 24 and `@shopify/cli@4.9.4`), `supabase/migrations/20261106204423_store_theme_access.sql` (new; pushed with the owner's permission), tests `test_shopify_theme.py` (new, 9), `test_publish_editable.py` (new, 2), `test_editor_map.py`; web `components/features/settings/shopify.tsx` (Theme Access), `components/features/builds/publish-controls.tsx` and `publish-context.tsx` (Editable sections, Edit in Shopify), `app/api/integrations/shopify/theme-access/route.ts` (new), `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/validators/publishing.ts`, `types/database.ts`; docs D121, BUILD_SPEC §12.6, MILESTONES, DESIGN, DEPENDENCIES, CLAUDE.md §5.
- **Schema changes:** `store_connections.theme_secret_id`, `theme_last4`. **New dependencies:** Shopify CLI 4.9.4 and Node 24 in the worker image (about 200 MB).
- **Decisions:** D121 locked.

  - one migration after all, for the password's Vault id, as the other secrets have;
  - the Edit in Shopify link opens the template, not the page;
  - the 600-blocks-a-section split is gone, because static blocks don't count toward Shopify's block limits;
  - pictures inside a text stay part of that text.

Shopify's theme checker found no Liquid or schema errors. A full publish through the website's endpoint succeeded in 27 s, and its test draft was then deleted.

### B4.3 — The layout check for editable Shopify pages
- **Status:** complete (owner: "1. yes. 2. yes. 3. yes." to the plan and "go with the next build", 2026-11-06; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Summary:** An editable Shopify page now keeps the page's layout: columns side by side, sticky parts and the spacing between sections.
  - **The check** (`publish/parity`): Shopify's render of LP's own theme files is worked out locally (`engines/shopify_render`, no Shopify call) and compared with the page in Chromium at desktop 1280, tablet 768 and phone 390, the sticky parts also at a third and two thirds down; boxes match within 4 px or 1 % of the width.
  - **Fixing the split:** shared wrappers that only group things become transparent; shared containers holding sections side by side are kept together as one Shopify section, its parts as group blocks (nested static blocks, each still editable); up to three more tries; then coarse.
  - **Every text is one whole block** (its element drawn by the block), so Shopify's outline and spacing sit on the element.
  - **Every editable publish** runs the check and records the mode with the publication.
  - **The template editor's Shopify editing card** has **Check layout** (job `shopify_layout_check`, 10–20 s, free), which keeps the result with the template's editor map and says when the page changed since; `app.cli.shopify_check` checks every template.
  - **Result:** every template with sections matches at all three widths (fixture and top 5: one section per LP section; the wear test (BRĒZ): 5 sections with the article and its card kept together; listicle and the cardio clone: one). FourU accepted a published copy with group blocks and selection markers (34 s).
- **Same-day follow-ups (owner):**
  - Buddy's section chip, in the composer and in sent requests, as the file chip in Claude Code's chat: 11 px regular, 6 / 4 px padding, 4 px corners, 24 px tall, the section icon at 8 px, Foreground-01 with a thin Stroke-Strong ring and a soft shadow all round (`shadow-chip`); sent requests have 8 px padding on every side.
  - The sections drawer's line against the preview is back (a 1 px Stroke-Strong line above the content).
  - The page editor's Undo and Redo as Shopify draws them: one pill, two icon-only halves (`HistoryButtons`, tokens `icon-strong` and `icon-off`).
  - The page editor's top bar laid out as Shopify's: the exit icon and Sections on the left, the page icon, the title (cut shorter, without the key), a divider and the save status centred, the viewport and Undo / Redo on the right (`ExitIcon`, `PageFileIcon`).
  - `cn` now knows `shadow-chip` (it had been dropping a custom size class as a colour).
  - A study of Shopify's theme editor from the owner's recording (`docs/reference/shopify-editor-study.md`), for the next build.

### B4.4 — The section tree, after Shopify's theme editor
- **Status:** complete (owner: "1. use what shopify uses. 2. yes. 3. yes. 4. yes." to the plan, 2026-11-07; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Summary:** You can now pick any single text, picture or video in the page editor, as in Shopify's theme editor, and point Buddy at just that part.
  - **The tree:** each section in the sections drawer opens into its parts, named as Shopify names their blocks ("Paragraph — *Most cocktail nights…*", "Headline", "Button", "Scene picture"), from the editor map (`GET /builds/{id}/parts`, per page version). Drag still reorders sections.
  - **Shared hover:** hovering a row outlines the part on the page with a tab naming it. Hovering the page lights the row.
  - **Picking:** a row, or the first click on a line on the page, picks the part (outline and name tab on the page, the row blue, its panel). A second click, or a double click, types on the page. Pictures still open the image studio; a section row opens the section's panel. Nothing is outlined until something is picked.
  - **The panel:** the tree shrinks to a strip scrolled to the row; the panel fills the room under it, framed as Buddy's change card, and arrives as Buddy's panel does. A part's words can be edited there: they show on the page as you type and save as typing does.
  - **Buddy on one part:** the chip names the part, the request says which line or picture, and Buddy is told to change only that. The panel's **Ask Buddy to edit** and an **Ask Buddy** pill under the picked part on the page open Buddy on it.
- **Same-session requests (owner):**
  - the template editor's Undo / Redo now use the same Shopify-style pill (the old ShortcutButton is gone);
  - the Sections button in the top bar uses the owner's sections icon (`SectionsNavIcon`) and the tree the owner's section icon (`SectionIcon`), both drawn with the line and margin of the icons beside them;
  - the motion of Shopify's panel measured frame by frame (`docs/reference/shopify-editor-study.md`).
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/editor_map.py` (`parts`; Shopify's block names moved here), `app/engines/shopify_theme.py` (uses them), `app/editor_parts.py` (new: the map for a build, the parts), `app/main.py` (`GET /builds/{id}/parts`), `app/models/editor_map.py` (`MapPart`, `SectionParts`, `PageParts`), `app/models/agent.py` (`AgentPart`, `target_part`), `app/agent.py` (checks and keeps the part), `app/jobs/kinds/agent_turn.py` (`_about`; mock on the line), `app/jobs/kinds/publish_shopify_editable.py` (shares the map loader), tests `test_editor_map.py`, `test_agent.py`; web `components/features/page-editor/section-panel.tsx` (the tree, the cards), `page-editor.tsx` (parts query, picking, Buddy's target), `page-canvas.tsx` (picked part, hover, names, panel typing), `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts` (first click picks, tabs, the pill, live text), `lander/composer-parts.tsx` (the part chip), `lib/pages/lander.ts`, `lib/validators/{desk,agent}.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `app/api/builds/[buildId]/parts/route.ts` (new), `components/builds/desk-context.tsx`, `components/ui/{icons.ts,lp-icons.tsx}`, `components/app/history-buttons.tsx`, `editor-top-bar.tsx`, `templates/editor/template-editor.tsx`, `page-editor.test.tsx`; docs D123, BUILD_SPEC §11.2, MILESTONES, DESIGN, the Shopify editor study.

  - the panel is framed as Buddy's change card and arrives as Buddy's panel (owner, during the build), not Shopify's slide-up;
  - parts outside the marked sections aren't in the tree (they can still be picked on the page);
  - a section row opens the section's panel (its move, duplicate, remove) in the same card.
- **Verification:** see the Build Report (typecheck, lint, web and worker tests, and the editor driven in a browser on a temporary copy of the BRĒZ page in light and dark: parts listed in about 2 s with line numbers matching the editor's; the split measured frame by frame; first click picks and the second types; panel typing shown live and saved; the pill opens Buddy with the part chip).
- **Follow-up (owner, same day, from a second recording of Shopify's sidebar):** the grip takes the section icon's place on hover (its own grey square), the chevron has its own hover square, parts start under the section's icon with picture thumbnails, and the tree's foot fades so rows flow under the card (`scroll-fade-b`).
- **Follow-up (owner: "you can add those"):** Shopify's eye hides and shows a section, line or picture (one undoable change each way; `HideAction`, `engines/visibility`; hidden rows dimmed with the crossed-out eye; the part left out everywhere the page goes); a lock in place of a part's icon on hover. The "+" line and Add section were built and then taken out at the owner's word (no section library yet). Noted: every desk change takes 10 to 17 s on this machine's dev setup (saving the version and reading the editor's state again; the page change itself takes 0.03 s), the same for typing, moving and formatting, worth a speed pass.

### B4.5 — Speed pass (the editor's saves and loads)
- **Status:** complete (owner: "optimize the app", then "go" on the plan, 2026-11-07; not yet committed)
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Summary:** Changes in the page editor and opening it now wait on far fewer network round trips.
  - **Storage:** one HTTP client kept open for the whole process (repeat downloads 1.0 → 0.24 s here).
  - **Database:** the build, its running page job and its template's rules in one query; undo and redo snapshots in one; the editor's state in one query outside a transaction (`db.read`).
  - **After a change:** the state is built from the page just written (no second download), and the replaced files are deleted after the answer, in the background.
  - **Web:** the page editor no longer rebuilds its route on the server after each change (the answer carries the new state); the review desk still does.
- **Measured from this Mac, on a copy of BRĒZ:**

  | | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Read the editor's state | 4.0 s | 0.55 s |
  | Hide or show a section | 11.4 s | 2.0 s |
  | Typing save | (slow, "Saving…" for 6 s or more) | 1.9 s |
  | Click to screen in the browser | 11 s or more, plus a route rebuild | about 3 s, no rebuild |

  On Railway each database trip is about 20 ms (beside the database), so the live app gains less in seconds but the same in trips. The website's functions already run in Dublin (`dub1`), beside the database.
- **Key files:** worker `app/providers/storage.py` (shared client), `app/db.py` (`read`), `app/desk.py` (`_load` with the busy job and rules, `_all_snapshots`, `_colors_from`, `desk_state(html=…)`, `_finish` cleanup in the background, `_rules_of`), `app/images.py`, `app/agent.py`; tests `test_desk.py` (the state after a change equals a fresh read), `test_share.py` (a minute's leeway for the database's clock); web `components/features/builds/desk-context.tsx` (`refreshRoute`), `app/(studio)/pages/[buildId]/editor/page.tsx`; docs D124, MILESTONES.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D124 locked.
- **Deviations from the plan:** none. Also noted: each website API call looks up the workspace membership in the database (one trip; a few ms in Dublin), left as it is.
- **Verification:** see the Build Report.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** to agree with the owner.

### B5.1 — Hardening: publishing and the B4 regressions
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go and yes", the migration push permitted, 2026-11-07; committed and pushed in 4a26510, 2026-11-08)
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Summary:** The first build of M5 Hardening, from the state-machine audit.
  - **Only the approved version goes out:** each publish job checks the page is still the version approved when Publish was pressed; an edit since stops it with "approve it again", and nothing is sent.
  - **A succeeded publish stays succeeded:** a worker stopping mid-publish releases the job (no false failure, no duplicate Shopify draft).
  - **No publish stuck on "running":** a database trigger marks a publication failed when its job fails, is cancelled or is reaped.
  - **Remove drafts for editable Shopify pages:** deletes every editable draft page still in the store, so the page can go to Trash.
  - **No overwritten words:** the part panel and the review desk's line editor save against the words they showed.
  - **Page and tree in step:** the page scrolls to every tree pick; clicking empty space on the page clears the tree's pick.
  - **Found while checking:** the worker refused fresh sign-ins for a few seconds when the machine's clock was behind Supabase's; it now allows 30 s.
- **Key files:** worker `app/jobs/kinds/publish_vercel.py`, `publish_shopify.py` (also Remove drafts), `publish_shopify_editable.py`, `app/publishing.py` (`approved_as_pressed`, `EDITED_SINCE`, editable unpublish, editable state), `app/models/publishing.py`, `app/auth.py` (leeway), `supabase/migrations/20261107034055_publication_job_failed.sql` (new, pushed with the owner's permission), tests `test_publish_editable.py` (`test_hardened_publishing`); web `components/features/builds/publish-controls.tsx` (Remove drafts), `publish-context.tsx`, `app/api/builds/[buildId]/unpublish/route.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `components/features/page-editor/section-panel.tsx`, `page-canvas.tsx`, `page-editor.tsx`, `components/features/builds/desk-panel.tsx`, `app/preview/[buildId]/route.ts`, tests `publish-controls.test.tsx`, `page-editor.test.tsx`; docs D125, MILESTONES (M5), BUILD_SPEC §12.
- **Schema changes:** a trigger, `jobs_publication_failed`. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D125 locked.
- **Deviations from the plan:** the worker's 30 s token leeway (found while checking; it made the editor fall back to the review page right after signing in).
- **Verification:** see the Build Report.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B5.2, stuck states and background jobs (plan first).

### B5.2 — Hardening: stuck states and background jobs
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go with your recommendation", 2026-11-07; committed in 89ed5a1)
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Summary:**
  - **The page can't stay locked:** a rewrite or Buddy request no worker claims in 15 minutes ends as failed, and the page unlocks.
  - **Buddy turns always finish:** a turn settles from its own job if the desk's settling was skipped.
  - **Approval waits:** approving is refused while an AI picture, a product match fix or a phone fix is changing the page, saying what to wait for.
  - **Pictures:** the "Making a new picture…" spinner can't come back after the picture lands; one picture at a time.
  - **Trash holds:** importing a product that's in Trash asks for a restore; pages from a template in Trash keep working.
  - **Double starts:** Buddy, QA and product checks started twice at once answer "already running".
- **Key files:** worker `app/jobs/queue.py` (`expire_unclaimed`), `app/jobs/runner.py`, `app/desk.py` (`_background` in `set_status`), `app/jobs/kinds/product_pull.py`, `app/pipeline/nodes/load_inputs.py`, `app/agent.py`, `app/qa.py`, `app/fidelity.py`, tests `test_jobs.py`, `test_desk.py`, `test_product_jobs.py`; web `components/features/page-editor/lander/use-lander.ts`, `page-editor.tsx`, `image-panel.tsx`, test `image-panel.test.tsx`; docs D126, MILESTONES.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D126 locked.
- **Deviations from the plan:** importing a trashed product stops with a message rather than updating it quietly in Trash (clearer; the plan said it would stay in Trash, which it does). A pipeline test that expected a queued build to fail when its template went to Trash now checks it finishes (the new rule).
- **Verification:** see the Build Report.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B5.3, direct database writes (plan first).

### B3.5c — Builds panel frames
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go" to the plan and its three recommendations, 2026-11-08; committed and pushed with B6.3, 2026-11-08)
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Summary:**
  - **Each page in a batch's Builds panel** is now its build happening, not a list of steps: the header as before (the template and the count), the build page's illustration following that page's build, and a footer card with the spinner, the step it's on in words that morph, and a chevron that unfolds every step.

### B6.6 — Settings speed
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go", 2026-11-08; committed and pushed, 2026-11-08)
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Summary:**
  - **Measured live:** content 510–580 ms after opening a settings page (Connections 930 ms), first visits about 0.9 s, cold switches 410–520 ms, from here (about 130 ms from Vercel). The website's functions are in Dublin beside the database; sign-in checks are local (ES256).
  - **The worker's share** was about 150 ms per read, mostly round trips: its membership check, on every request, opened a transaction (3 round trips where 1 does), and so did the members, integrations and Trash reads.
  - **Fixed:** those now read without a transaction: Members about 8 round trips → 4, General and Connections 8 → 4, Trash 9 → 3, every worker request 2 fewer (about 20 ms each on Railway).
  - **Also, from the owner's messages during the build:** Back to app sits level with the header's "Settings" (measured equal); the invite modal's Role menu is the field's colour.
  - **After the deploy (measured live, 2026-11-08):** the worker's own time per read fell from about 140–195 ms to 58–83 ms (Members 83, Integrations 79, Trash 58). Whole settings pages timed from the developer's machine vary by a few hundred milliseconds from run to run (distance and connection set-up), more than the saving, so they don't show it cleanly.
  - **Tried and reverted:** prefetching the settings links fully (and on hover): Next leaves pages that read the session after `connection()` out of prefetches, so it added requests without making switches faster.
- **Key files:** worker `app/auth.py`, `app/members.py`, `app/publishing.py`, `app/trash.py`; web `components/app/settings-sidebar.tsx`, `components/ui/select.tsx`; docs D135, MILESTONES.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D135 locked.
- **Deviations from the plan:** the session-read change that instant switches would need is left to the owner (app-wide, more server work per visible link).
- **Verification:** see the Build Report.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B6.7 profile photo, then B3.5d the build page's steps frame.

### B3.1i — Buddy polish, and the lag fix
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go", 2026-11-08; committed and pushed, 2026-11-08)
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Summary:**
  - **The lag:** B3.1h had Buddy set his eyes' sizes and colours on every frame, so each mouse move made the browser redo layout once per Buddy on screen. He's back to transforms only (sizes and colours only while morphing or listening); measured, his own work now causes no layout.

  - **"Listening…"** with the app's shimmer, in the bar and Buddy's panel.
  - **The recorder:** keeps listening until Stop (the browser ends sessions after a pause; it starts a new one), keeps words still being heard, and says why when it can't listen.

### B7.3a — Change a page's offer, a batch's, and variants
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go", 2026-11-10; committed and pushed, 2026-11-10)
- **Date:** 2026-11-10
- **Summary:**
  - **Change offer…** on the build page: pick another offer (or none); the prices, badges, promo line, options and buttons follow at once, and Claude rewrites only the lines that state the old deal, as one undo step; the window shows each changed line before and after, and anything to check.
  - **Guards** (agreed before building): code finds the mentions (including what the page's own price and badge slots said); other lines must come back exactly as they were or nothing saves; a line still stating the old deal gets a second try; old facts left, invented prices or percents, and rewrites that dropped more than the deal are listed; untouched lines that sound like a deal are listed to look at.
  - **A batch:** Change offer on the batch screen, for the ticked pages (variants with another offer unticked), each with its progress and result.
  - **Variants:** Duplicate with offers… on every page ⋯: one copy per ticked offer, each changed; the tree reads "Copy 2 · Ten off".
  - **Builds hide sale parts** without a discount instead of removing them, so a later offer can show them; parts hidden by hand stay hidden.
- **Key files:** worker `app/engines/offers.py` (facts, page_facts, mentions, leftovers, invented, dropped, deal_hint, apply_to_page, show_for_offer), `app/jobs/kinds/offer_apply.py` (new), `app/offer_change.py` (new), `app/engines/copy_rules.py` (offer_prompt), `app/engines/finalize.py` (hide, not remove), `app/desk.py` (the page job), `app/builds.py` + `app/models/build.py` (batch offer fields), `app/main.py` (endpoints), `app/jobs/registry.py`; tests `test_offers.py`, `test_offer_change.py` (new), `test_page_engines.py`, `test_template_import.py`; web `components/features/offers/change-offer-dialog.tsx`, `variants-dialog.tsx`, `batch-offer-dialog.tsx`, `offer-result.tsx` (new, + `offer-windows.test.tsx`), `lib/queries/offers.ts` (new), routes `api/builds/[buildId]/offer`, `api/builds/[buildId]/variants`, `api/builds/batch/[batchId]/offer`, `api/brands/[brandId]/offers` (new), `components/features/builds/page-menu.tsx`, `pages/page-row-menu.tsx`, `pages/pages-list.tsx`, `builds/batch-view.tsx`, `redraft-controls.tsx`, `lib/pages/list.ts` (copyLabel), `lib/builds/queries.ts`, `lib/builds/desk.ts`, `lib/validators/offer.ts`, `build.ts`, `desk.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/jobs/job-titles.json`; docs D147, MILESTONES, DESIGN.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D147.
- **Deviations:** the "sound like a deal" lines are found by code (deal words), not flagged by Claude (the reply format is one rewrite per line; code is predictable and free). Added after the live check: the "dropped more than the deal" check (a rewrite lost "a straightforward return policy") and old facts read from the page's own slots (pages built before offers show other prices than their recorded offer gives).
- **Verification:** see the Build Report.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** B7.3b (Approve and publish all), on the owner's go.

### B7.3b — Approve and publish all
- **Status:** complete (owner: "continue the next build phase", 2026-11-10)
- **Date:** 2026-11-10
- **Summary:**
  - **A batch's screen:** Approve and publish all in the header, and a "2 pages to review" bar with the same action while pages wait.
  - **The window** lists each page with what will happen: approve and go live again where it was live, publish again, approve only (never live, or can't go live, with why), nothing, or skipped; ticks what it can act on; one Publish anyway for checks that may be passed.
  - **Starting it** approves the chosen pages, sends each brand's Vercel pages out in one deployment (so they go live together and can't overwrite each other) and each Shopify page on its own; a page that can't go says why, the others go on; the window follows each page to Live, or why not with Try again.

### Fixes after B7.3b — Trash on the batch screen, a variant's offer at once, a wider page menu
- **Status:** complete (owner, 2026-11-11)
- **Date:** 2026-11-11
- **Summary:**
  - **Move to Trash on a batch's screen:** the row leaves at once, and comes back on Undo, without a reload (that list is kept by React Query, which the page refresh didn't read again; `PageRowMenu`/`MoveToTrashDialog` take `onChanged`).
  - **Duplicate with offers:** each copy names its offer as it's made, so the lists say "Copy 3 · Ten off" at once instead of after a reload. The `offer_apply` job is told the deal the page still states (`from_offer`), and if the change fails the copy names that deal again (by id only while that offer still exists).
  - **A page's ⋯ menus** (rows and the build page) are 160 px (`w-menu-page`), so "Duplicate with offers…" sits on one line.
- **Key files:** worker `app/offer_change.py` (`variants`, `put_back`), `app/jobs/kinds/offer_apply.py`, `tests/test_offer_change.py`; web `components/features/pages/page-row-menu.tsx`, `components/features/trash/move-to-trash-dialog.tsx`, `components/features/builds/batch-view.tsx`, `components/features/builds/page-menu.tsx`, `app/globals.css`.
- **Schema changes:** none. **New dependencies:** none.
- **Verification:** worker ruff, format and mypy clean; `test_offer_change.py` 4 passed (new: a variant names its offer at once, and gets the old one back when its change fails); web typecheck, lint, tokens, prettier, 333 tests pass. In the browser (throwaway account, mock worker): menu 160 px with every item on one line; "Copy 3 · Ten off" showed with the toast, no reload; a trashed row left at once and came back on Undo; the copy's offer job succeeded. Account deleted afterwards.

### B7.4 — Update every page using an offer
- **Status:** complete (owner: "go", 2026-11-11)
- **Date:** 2026-11-11
- **Summary:**
  - **After an offer is edited:** if pages selling it now say something it no longer does, the edit window becomes **Update the N pages that use it**: the out-of-date pages ticked, the others saying why not, "About 2¢ a page", **Update N pages** or **Not now**.
  - **Updating** is Change offer's job on each page, from its record of the offer to the offer now ("Updated Ten off to its new terms", one undo step, back to review); a page already up to date is left alone.
  - **The window follows each page** (place in line, step, "Updated · 3 lines rewritten, 1 to check" with Check it, or why not with Try again; "6 of 20 updated"), can be closed and reopened from the offer's ⋯ **Update pages…**, and ends with **Approve and publish all** for the offer's pages (any batch).
  - **The Offers tab** row says "3 pages · 2 out of date" or "Updating 14 pages…". Renaming an offer needs no update: the lists show its current name.
  - **Risks handled:** out of date is defined once, in the database (`offer_out_of_date`, `brand_offer_usage`); Undo and Redo put the page's offer record back with the page (`build_snapshots.offer`), for Change offer, Duplicate with offers and the update; the wait is shown and walking away is safe.
- **Key files:** migration `supabase/migrations/20261111081446_offer_updates.sql`, pgTAP `supabase/tests/database/offer_updates.test.sql`; worker `app/offer_change.py` (`pages_using`, `update_pages`), `app/desk.py` (`offer_record`, `put_offer`, Undo/Redo), `app/publish_all.py` (any list of pages; skips pages being changed), `app/jobs/kinds/offer_apply.py`, `app/builds.py`, `app/main.py` (`/offers/{id}/pages`, `/offers/{id}/publish-all`), tests `test_offer_change.py`, `test_publish.py`; web `components/features/offers/update-pages-dialog.tsx` (+ test, new), `offers-view.tsx` (+ test), `offer-modal.tsx` (note), `builds/publish-all-dialog.tsx` (a batch's or an offer's pages), `app/api/offers/[offerId]/pages` and `/publish-all` (new), `lib/offers/{queries,actions,form}.ts`, `lib/builds/queries.ts`, `lib/api/worker.ts`, `lib/validators/offer.ts`, `types/database.ts` (regenerated).
- **Schema changes:** `build_snapshots.offer` (jsonb); functions `offer_out_of_date(jsonb, offers)` and `brand_offer_usage(uuid)` (members only). Applied to the cloud dev project (`pnpm db:push`), types regenerated.
- **New dependencies:** none.
- **Decisions:** D149.
- **Deviations:** none. Found while checking: the page list's name for a copy is "Copy N" (as the batch windows), and the runner's first step reads "Starting".
- **Verification:** worker ruff, format, mypy clean, pytest 536 passed and 1 failed: the tenant-isolation pgTAP, broken by a test workspace an interrupted earlier run left behind (removed; `pnpm test:db` then 7 passed). Web typecheck, lint, tokens, prettier, vitest 337 passed (the update window's tests again after the last polish), `pnpm e2e` passed. In the browser (throwaway account, mock worker, deleted afterwards): editing Ten off opened Update the 3 pages, Not now left "3 pages · 3 out of date", Update pages… reopened it, the pages went Waiting · 1st in line → Starting → Updated, then Approve and publish all listed them.
- **Owner confirmed:** no
- **Next:** the owner tests B7.3b, the fixes after it, and B7.4 together; then M8 (A/B testing), on the owner's go.
